                                    |\n    31→| --------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n    32→| Social Learning       | Cohort discussions, live tutoring rooms, activity feed, and gamified achievements                    |\n    33→| Realtime Events       | Event-driven micro-services orchestrated via Kafka/NATS                                              |\n    34→| Adaptive Content      | Personalized recommendations based on learner behavior & mastery maps                                |\n    35→| Admin Dashboard       | Live operational metrics, content moderation, role-based access control                              |\n    36→| Enterprise Grade      | SSL/TLS everywhere, granular session management, audit-ready logs, GDPR support                      |\n    37→\n    38→---\n    39→\n    40→## Architecture\n    41→\n    42→```mermaid\n    43→flowchart LR\n    44→  subgraph Front-End (SPA)\n    45→    FE1[React <br/> Redux/RTK]\n    46→    FE2[Next.js SSR]\n    47→  end\n    48→\n    49→  subgraph API Gateway\n    50→    GW[Node.js <br/> Express]\n    51→  end\n    52→\n    53→  subgraph Micro-services\n    54→    AUTH[Auth Service]\n    55→    LEARN[Learning Service]\n    56→    FEED[Activity Feed]\n    57→    NOTIF[Notification]\n    58→    GAMIFY[Gamification]\n    59→    SEARCH[Search/Indexer]\n    60→  end\n    61→\n    62→  subgraph Infra\n    63→    NATS[NATS JetStream]\n    64→    DB[(PostgreSQL Cluster)]\n    65→    CACHE[(Redis Cluster)]\n    66→    FS[(S3/MinIO)]\n    67→    ES[(OpenSearch)]\n    68→  end\n    69→\n    70→  FE1 -- HTTPS --> GW\n    71→  FE2 -- HTTPS --> GW\n    72→  GW --> AUTH\n    73→  GW --> LEARN\n    74→  GW --> FEED\n    75→\n    76→  AUTH <-->|events| NATS\n    77→  LEARN <-->|events| NATS\n    78→  FEED <-->|events| NATS\n    79→  NOTIF <-->|events| NATS\n    80→  GAMIFY <-->|events| NATS\n    81→  SEARCH <-->|events| NATS\n    82→\n    83→  LEARN -- SQL --> DB\n    84→  AUTH -- SQL --> DB\n    85→  FEED -- SQL --> DB\n    86→  LEARN -- S3 --> FS\n    87→  SEARCH -- Index --> ES\n    88→  NOTIF -- Pub/Sub --> CACHE\n    89→```\n    90→\n    91→Design patterns in play: **Service Layer**, **Repository**, **MVC Controllers**, **Domain Events**, **CQRS**, and **Outbox** pattern for transactional event publishing.\n    92→\n    93→---\n    94→\n    95→## Tech Stack\n    96→\n    97→| Layer               | Tech                                                                                              |\n    98→| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n    99→| Front-End           | React 18, Next.js 13, Docker & Docker Compose ≥ v2.20\n   115→* `pnpm` (preferred) or `npm`\n   116→* Optional: `nats-server`, `kafka`, `psql` installed locally for bare-metal runs\n   117→\n   118→### Quick Local Run (Docker)\n   119→\n   120→```bash\n   121→git clone https://github.com/pulselearn/campus-hub.git\n   122→cd campus-hub\n   123→\n   124→# Spin up DB, NATS, search, redis, and local MinIO\n   125→docker compose up -d infra\n   126→\n   127→# Install dependencies\n   128→pnpm install --filter=./apps/web_social\n   129→\n   130→# Start API Gateway + services\n   131→pnpm --filter @pulselearn/web_social run dev\n   132→```\n   133→\n   134→Access web UI at `https://localhost:3000`, API at `https://localhost:4000/api`.\n   135→\n   136→### Environment Variables\n   137→\n   138→Create `.env` at project root (see `.env.example`):\n   139→\n   140→```dotenv\n   141→DATABASE_URL=postgresql://plch_user:secret@localhost:5432/pulselearn\n   142→NATS_URI=nats://localhost:4222\n 147→S3_ENDPOINT=http://localhost:9000\n   148→S3_ACCESS_KEY=minio\n   149→S3_SECRET_KEY=miniopass\n   150→```\n   151→\n   152→---\n   153→\n   154→## Project Structure\n   155→\n   156→```\n   157→pulselearn-campus-hub/\n   158→├── apps/\n   159→│   ├── api-gateway/      # GraphQL + REST BFF\n   160→│   ├── web_social/       # Front-end SPA/SSR\n   161→│   ├── services/\n   162→│   │   ├── client with Avro schemas\n   172→│   └── ui-kit/           # component library\n   173→└── infra/\n   174→    └── docker/           # compose, k8s manifests, helm charts\n   175→```\n   176→\n   177→---\n   178→\n   179→## Domain Events & Message Flow\n   180→\n   181→Each domain action emits an immutable event.  \n   182→Events are versioned, schema-registered, and can be replayed for new projections.\n   183→\n   184→| Event Name              | Payload (key fields)              | Trigger                          |\n   185→| ----------------------- | --------------------------------- | -------------------------------- |\n   186→| `AssignmentSubmitted`   | `assignmentId`, `userId`, `file`  | Student uploads assignment       |\n   187→| `BadgeAwarded`          | `badgeId`, `userId`, `reason`     | Gamification service decision    |\n   188→| `SessionExpired`        | `userId`, `expiresAt`             | Auth service TTL watcher         |\n   189→| `LectureUploaded`       | `lectureId`, `courseId`           | Instructor uploads lecture       |\n   190→| `CoursePaid`            | `orderId`, `userId`, `amount`     | Stripe webhook                   |\n   191→\n   192→Example Avro schema:\n   193→\n   194→```jsonc\n   195→{\n   196→  \"namespace\": \"com.pulselearn.events\",\n   197→  \"type\": \"record\",\n   198→  \"name\": \"BadgeAwarded_v1\",\n   199→  \"fields\": [\n   200→    { \"name\": \"badgeId\", \"type\": \"string\" },\n   201→    { \"name\": \"userId\",  \"type\": \"string\" },\n   202→    { \"name\": \"reason\",  \"type\": \"string\" },\n   203→    { \"name\": \"awardedAt\", \"type\": { \"type\": \"long\", \"logicalType\": \"timestamp-millis\" } }\n   204→  ]\n   205→}\n   206→```\n   207→\n   208→---\n   209→\n   210→## Security & Compliance\n   211→\n   212→* TLS 1.3 enforced end-to-end (Let’s Encrypt in dev, ACM in prod)  \n   213→* Argon2id passwords, JWTs signed with 4096-bit RSA keys  \n   214→* OWASP Top-10 hardened (rate-limiting, input validation via Zod)  \n   215→* GDPR: Right-to-be-Forgotten endpoints, data export, opt-in analytics  \n   216→* Audit logs streamed to OpenSearch, immutable S3 with object locking  \n   217→\n   218→---\n   219→\n   220→## Scripts & Tooling\n   221→\n   222→| Script                       | Purpose                                  |\n   223→| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |\n   224→| `pnpm dev`                   | Hot-reload dev server (nodemon + ts-node)|\n   225→| `pnpm build`                 | Compile TypeScript, bundle assets        |\n   226→| `pnpm test`                  | Run unit & integration tests             |\n   227→| `pnpm lint`                  | ESLint linting                           |\n   228→| `pnpm format`                | Prettier formatting                      |\n   229→| `docker compose up infra`    | Spin up databases & brokers              |\n   230→| `helm upgrade --install`     | Deploy to Kubernetes                     |\n   231→\n   232→Continuous Integration is handled by GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) which runs:\n   233→\n   234→1. Type checking  \n   235→2. ESLint + Prettier  \n   236→3. Jest with code coverage  \n   237→4. Container build & vulnerability scanning (Trivy)  \n   238→\n   239→---\n   240→\n   241→## Contributing\n   242→\n   243→1. Dynamic Nginx Configuration Builder\n     4→ * -----------------------------------------------------------\n     5→ * This script is responsible for generating a robust, battle-tested\n     6→ * nginx.conf file that fronts the API-Gateway of the PulseLearn platform.\n     7→ *\n     8→ * Why JavaScript for an “nginx.conf”?\n     9→ * ----------------------------------\n    10→ * • Configuration needs to adapt to dynamic container / k8s deployments.\n    11→ * • Runtime values (hostnames, ports, ssl paths) come from ENV vars.\n    12→ * • We want validation, helpful error messages and unit-testability.\n    13→ *\n    14→ * Usage\n    15→ * -----\n    16→ *  $ node nginx.conf [output-path]\n    17→ *\n    18→ *  – If executed via `node`, the generated configuration is written to\n    19→ *    the provided output path (default: /etc/nginx/conf.d/pulselearn.conf)\n    20→ *  – When imported (`require('./nginx.conf')`) the `buildNginxConfig`\n    21→ *    method returns the config as a string.\n    22→ */\n    23→\n    24→'use strict';\n    25→\n    26→const fs   = require('fs');\n    27→const path = require('path');\n    28→const os   = require('os');\n    29→\n    30→////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n    31→// Environment Schema\n    32→////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n    33→\n    34→const ENV_SCHEMA = {\n    35→  /** Comma-separated hosts for each micro-service.\n    36→   *  Example: AUTH_SERVICE_HOSTS=\"auth1:4000,auth2:4000\" */\n    37→  AUTH_SERVICE_HOSTS        : { required: true  },\n    38→  USER_SERVICE_HOSTS        : { required: true  },\n    39→  CONTENT_SERVICE_HOSTS     : { required: true  },\n    40→  NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_HOSTS: { required: true  },\n    41→\n    42→  /** SSL certificate / key */\n    43→  SSL_CERT_PATH             : { required: false, default: '/etc/ssl/certs/pulselearn.crt' },\n    44→  SSL_KEY_PATH              : { required: false, default: '/etc/ssl/private/pulselearn.key' },\n    45→\n    46→  /** Optional toggles */\n    47→  FORCE_SSL                 : { required: false, default: 'true' },\n    48→  CLIENT_MAX_BODY_SIZE_MB   : { required: false, default: '20' },\n    49→  RATE_LIMIT_REQ_PER_MIN    : { required: false, default: '600' },\n    50→\n    51→  /** Logging */\n    52→  ACCESS_LOG_PATH           : { required: false, default: '/var/log/nginx/access.log' },\n    53→  ERROR_LOG_PATH            : { required: false, default: '/var/log/nginx/error.log' }\n    54→};\n    55→\n    56→/**\n    57→ * Load and validate environment variables against the schema.\n    58→ * Throws if a required variable is missing.\n    59→ */\n    60→function loadEnv () {\n    61→  const conf = {};\n    62→\n    63→  Object.entries(ENV_SCHEMA).forEach(([key, meta]) => {\n    64→    const value = process.env[key] ?? meta.default;\n    65→    if (meta.required && (value === undefined || value === null)) {\n    66→      throw new Error(`Missing required environment variable: ${key}`);\n    67→    }\n    68→    conf[key] = value;\n    69→  });\n    70→\n    71→  return conf;\n    72→}\n    73→\n    74→////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n    75→// Helper Functions\n    76→////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n    77→\n    78→/**\n    79→ * Parse upstream hosts into nginx `server` directives.\n    80→ *\n    81→ * @param {string} hostsString - Comma-separated host:port pairs.\n    82→ * @returns {string}           - Multi-line string ready to embed.\n    83→ */\n    84→function toUpstreamServers (hostsString) {\n    85→  return hostsString\n    86→    .split(',')\n    87→    .map(host => host.trim())\n    88→    .filter(Boolean)\n    89→    .map(host => `        server ${host};`)\n    90→    .join('\\n');\n    91→}\n    92→\n    93→/**\n    94→ * Render a single upstream block.\n    95→ *\n    96→ * @param {string} name       - Upstream name (e.g. \"auth_service\").\n    97→ * @param {string} hosts      - Comma-separated list of host:port targets.\n    98→ * @param {object} [opts={}]  - Options like keepalive settings.\n    99→ */\n   100→function renderUpstream (name, hosts, opts = {}) {\n   101→  const keepalive = opts.keepalive ?? 64;\n   102→  return `\n   103→    upstream ${name} {\n   104→${toUpstreamServers(hosts)}\n   105→        keepalive ${keepalive};\n   106→    }`;\n   107→}\n   108→\n   109→/**\n   110→ * Render a location block that proxies to a given upstream service.\n   111→ *\n   112→ * @param {string} pathPrefix   - URI prefix (e.g. \"/auth/\").\n   113→ * @param {string} upstreamName - Name defined in an `upstream` block.\n   114→ */\n   115→function renderLocation (pathPrefix, upstreamName) {\n   116→  return `\n   117→            location ${pathPrefix} {\n   118→                proxy_pass          http://${upstreamName};\n   119→                proxy_http_version  1.1;\n   120→                proxy_set_header    Connection \"\";\n   121→                proxy_set_header    Host              $host;\n   122→                proxy_set_header    X-Real-IP         $remote_addr;\n   123→                proxy_set_header    X-Forwarded-For   $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;\n   124→                proxy_set_header    X-Forwarded-Proto $scheme;\n   125→                proxy_set_header    X-Request-ID      $request_id;\n   126→                proxy_cache_bypass  $cookie_session;\n   127→            }`;\n   128→}\n   129→\n   130→////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n   131→// Main Config Builder\n   132→////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n   133→\n   134→/**\n   135→ * Build the complete nginx.conf string.\n   136→ *\n   137→ * @returns {string} The final configuration.\n   138→ */\n   139→function buildNginxConfig () {\n   140→  const env = loadEnv();\n   141→\n   142→  const RATE_LIMIT_ZONE = `\n   143→    limit_req_zone $binary_remote_addr zone=req_limit:${parseInt(env.RATE_LIMIT_REQ_PER_MIN, 10) * 32}k rate=${env.RATE_LIMIT_REQ_PER_MIN}r/m;`;\n   144→\n   145→  const UPSTREAMS = [\n   146→    ['auth_service',          env.AUTH_SERVICE_HOSTS],\n   147→    ['user_service',          env.USER_SERVICE_HOSTS],\n   148→    ['content_service',       env.CONTENT_SERVICE_HOSTS],\n   149→    ['notification_service',  env.NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_HOSTS]\n   150→  ].map(([name, hosts]) => renderUpstream(name, hosts)).join('\\n');\n   151→\n   152→  const LOCATIONS =\n   153→      renderLocation('/auth/',         'auth_service')          +\n   154→      renderLocation('/users/',        'user_service')          +\n   155→      renderLocation('/content/',      'content_service')       +\n   156→      renderLocation('/notifications/','notification_service');\n   157→\n   158→  const SSL_CONFIG = (env.FORCE_SSL === 'true')\n   159→    ? `\n   160→        listen 443 ssl http2;\n   161→        ssl_certificate     ${env.SSL_CERT_PATH};\n   162→        ssl_certificate_key ${env.SSL_KEY_PATH};\n   163→        ssl_protocols       TLSv1.2 TLSv1.3;\n   164→        ssl_ciphers         HIGH:!aNULL:!MD5;\n   165→        ssl_prefer_server_ciphers on;\n   166→        ssl_session_cache   shared:SSL:10m;\n   167→        add_header Strict-Transport-Security \"max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains\" always;`\n   168→    : 'listen 80;';\n   169→\n   170→  const REDIRECT_HTTP_TO_HTTPS = (env.FORCE_SSL === 'true')\n   171→    ? `\n   172→    server {\n   173→        listen      80;\n   174→        server_name _;\n   175→        return 301 https://$host$request_uri;\n   176→    }`\n   177→    : '';\n   178→\n   179→  return `\n   180→###############################################################################\n   181→#  PulseLearn Campus Hub – Auto-Generated Nginx Configuration\n   182→#  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   183→#  This file was generated by services/api-gateway/nginx.conf (NodeJS script).\n   184→#  DO NOT EDIT MANUALLY. Changes will be overwritten on the next deployment.\n   185→###############################################################################\n   186→\n   187→worker_processes auto;\n   188→pid /run/nginx.pid;\n   189→events { worker_connections 1024; }\n   190→\n   191→${RATE_LIMIT_ZONE}\n   192→\n   193→http {\n   194→    include       mime.types;\n   195→    default_type  application/octet-stream;\n   196→\n   197→    # Logs --------------------------------------------------------------------\n   198→    access_log ${env.ACCESS_LOG_PATH} main;\n   199→    error_log  ${env.ERROR_LOG_PATH}  warn;\n   200→\n   201→    # Compression -------------------------------------------------------------\n   202→    gzip on;\n   203→    gzip_types text/plain text/css application/json application/javascript text/xml application/xml application/xml+rss text/javascript;\n   204→\n   205→    # Upstreams ---------------------------------------------------------------\n   206→${UPSTREAMS}\n   207→\n   208→    # API Gateway Server Block -------------------------------------------------\n   209→    server {\n   210→${SSL_CONFIG}\n   211→\n   212→        server_name _;  # catch-all\n   213→\n   214→        client_max_body_size ${env.CLIENT_MAX_BODY_SIZE_MB}M;\n   215→\n   216→        # Rate Limiting -------------------------------------------------------\n   217→        limit_req zone=req_limit burst=15 nodelay;\n   218→\n   219→        # Security Headers ----------------------------------------------------\n   220→        add_header X-Content-Type-Options nosniff;\n   221→        add_header X-Frame-Options DENY;\n   222→        add_header X-XSS-Protection \"1; mode=block\";\n   223→\n   224→        # Proxy Routes --------------------------------------------------------\n   225→${LOCATIONS}\n   226→\n   227→        # Fallback ------------------------------------------------------------\n   228→        location / {\n   229→            return 404;\n   230→        }\n   231→    }\n   232→\n   233→${REDIRECT_HTTP_TO_HTTPS}\n   234→}\n   235→`;\n   236→}\n   237→\n   238→////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n   239→// CLI Entrypoint\n   240→////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n   241→\n   242→if (require.main === module) {\n   243→  (async () => {\n   244→    try {\n   245→      const outputPath = process.argv[2] ?? '/etc/nginx/conf.d/pulselearn.conf';\n   246→      const config     = buildNginxConfig();\n   247→\n   248→      // Ensure destination directory exists\n   249→      fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(outputPath), { recursive: true });\n   250→\n   251→      // Write atomically\n   252→      const tmpFile = path.join(os.tmpdir(), `nginx-${Date.now()}.conf`);\n   253→      fs.writeFileSync(tmpFile, config, { encoding: 'utf8', mode: 0o644 });\n   254→      fs.renameSync(tmpFile, outputPath);\n   255→\n   256→      console.log(`✔︎ nginx.conf successfully written to ${outputPath}`);\n   257→    } catch (err) {\n   258→      console.error('✖︎ Failed to generate nginx.conf:', err.message);\n   259→      process.exitCode = 1;\n   260→    }\n   261→  })();\n   262→}\n   263→\n   264→////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n   265→// Exports (for unit-tests)\n   266→////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n   267→module.exports = { buildNginxConfig };\n   268→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012cVuCLy3SmgfWRRu2gGCyp","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→```markdown\n     2→# ADR 001 – Adopt a Polyglot, Event-Driven Microservices Architecture\n     3→\n     4→* Status: **Accepted**  \n     5→* Date: 2024-05-19  \n     6→* Deciders: Architecture Council (CTO, Lead Backend, Lead Frontend, DevOps)  \n     7→* Supersedes: N/A  \n     8→* See also: 002-database-strategy.md, 003-auth-identity.md\n     9→\n    10→---\n    11→\n    12→## 1. Context  \n    13→\n    14→PulseLearn Campus Hub (PL-CH) must enable:\n    15→\n    16→1. Real-time, bidirectional interactions between thousands of concurrent users (students, teachers, admins).  \n    17→2. Rapid feature evolution by autonomous domain-centric teams.  \n    18→3. Fault-tolerance and graceful degradation of non-critical functionality (e.g., delayed “BadgeAwarded” events must **not** block quiz submissions).  \n    19→4. Polyglot technology choices while enforcing a consistent ops/observability contract.  \n    20→5. Compliance with FERPA, GDPR, and internal audit requirements such as end-to-end traceability and PII redaction policies.\n    21→\n    22→The alternatives considered were:\n    23→\n    24→| Candidate                     | Pros                                                                                                    | Cons                                                                                                   |\n    25→| ----------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n    26→| A) Single-service (monolith)  | Simple to deploy, transactional consistency, easier local dev                                           | Scalability bottlenecks, slower team velocity, long-lived branches, risky deployments                  |\n    27→| B) Self-contained services    | Autonomy per bounded context, independent UI + API bundles                                              | Risk of UI duplication, heavier front-end orchestration                                                |\n    28→| **C) Microservices + Event Bus** | Loose coupling via events, horizontal scalability, team autonomy, tech polyglot, easier domain modeling | Requires distributed tracing, eventual consistency semantics, operational complexity                   |\n    29→| D) Serverless (FaaS only)     | Zero infra management, cost per execution                                                               | Cold starts, vendor lock-in, limited long-running connections, complex local testing                   |\n    30→\n    31→After evaluating traffic patterns, compliance constraints, and organisation structure (5 cross-functional squads), option **C** was chosen.\n    32→\n    33→---\n    34→\n    35→## 2. Decision  \n    36→\n    37→We will build **PulseLearn Campus Hub** as an **event-driven microservices system**:\n    38→\n    39→1. **Domain-Oriented Microservices**  \n    40→   • Each service owns a bounded context & data store (e.g., `UserProfile`, `LearningPath`, `Achievement`, `Notification`).  \n    41→   • Services communicate asynchronously via a Kafka compatible bus (NATS JetStream in staging, Confluent Cloud in prod).  \n    42→   • REST/gRPC APIs remain for synchronous “query” use-cases and external integrations (admin panel, LTI).  \n    43→\n    44→2. **Event Taxonomy**  \n    45→   • All domain events adopt the CloudEvents 1.0 spec.  \n    46→   • Topic naming: `pl.<boundedContext>.<eventName>.<version>` (e.g., `pl.achievement.badge_awarded.v1`).  \n    47→   • Payloads are validated with versioned Avro schemas stored in Schema Registry.  \n    48→\n    49→3. **Shared Platform Libraries**  \n    50→   • We ship an OSS-friendly **`@pl/platform`** JavaScript package that wraps NATS/Kafka producers & consumers, tracing, and idempotency utilities (see snippet below).  \n    51→   • Analogous libraries will exist for Python, Go, and Java to keep polyglot parity.  \n    52→\n    53→4. **Observability & Resilience**  \n    54→   • OpenTelemetry for distributed tracing, correlated logs, metrics (`traceId` + `spanId` pass via W3C `traceparent`).  \n    55→   • Circuit breakers (opossum) guard cross-service HTTP calls.  \n    56→   • Retry, DLQ, and “parking lot” patterns for poison messages.  \n    57→\n    58→5. **Security**  \n    59→   • mTLS between services (Linkerd service mesh).  \n    60→   • SPIFFE IDs for zero-trust identity.  \n    61→   • PII encrypted at rest (PostgreSQL + envelope encryption).  \n    62→\n    63→---\n    64→\n    65→## 3. Consequences  \n    66→\n    67→Positive:  \n    68→✓ Independent deployability enables <15 min release cadence per squad.  \n    69→✓ Horizontal scaling by workload (e.g., `RecommendationService` auto-scales via KEDA based on Kafka lag).  \n    70→✓ Failure isolation—`SearchIndexer` crash has no direct impact on “QuizCompletion” UX.  \n    71→\n    72→Negative:  \n    73→✗ Added operational overhead: CI/CD pipelines, playbooks, Terraform modules per service.  \n    74→✗ Data consistency becomes eventual; we must adopt Saga patterns for workflows like “PurchaseCourse → ActivateAccess → EmitInvoice”.  \n    75→✗ On-call complexity: PagerDuty rotations per squad, plus a shared SRE roster for platform components (Kafka, Linkerd, Istio ingress).\n    76→\n    77→---\n    78→\n    79→## 4. Implementation Sketch  \n    80→\n    81→### Mermaid: High-level view\n    82→\n    83→```mermaid\n    84→flowchart LR\n    85→    subgraph Frontend\n    86→        ReactApp[Web SPA]\n    87→        MobileApp[React Native]\n    88→    end\n    89→    ReactApp -->|REST: /api/v1| APIGateway\n    90→    MobileApp -->|GraphQL| APIGateway\n    91→    APIGateway --> AuthSvc[(Auth Service)]\n    92→    APIGateway --> UserSvc[(User Profile)]\n    93→    APIGateway --> CourseSvc[(Course Catalog)]\n    94→    style APIGateway fill:#f5f5f5,stroke:#333,stroke-width:1px\n    95→    \n    96→    %% Event Bus\n    97→    subgraph EventBus[\"NATS / Kafka\"]\n    98→        NATS[(JetStream Topic)]\n    99→    end\n   100→    \n   101→    CourseSvc -->|pl.course.uploaded| NATS\n   102→    UserSvc -->|pl.user.session_expired| NATS\n   103→    NATS --> SearchSvc[(Search Indexer)]\n   104→    NATS --> BadgeSvc[(Achievement)]\n   105→    NATS --> RecSvc[(Recommendation)]\n   106→```\n   107→\n   108→### Shared JS Event Library (`@pl/platform`)\n   109→\n   110→```javascript\n   111→// @pl/platform/lib/event-bus.js\n   112→import { connect, StringCodec } from 'nats';\n   113→import { v4 as uuid } from 'uuid';\n   114→import * as otel from '@opentelemetry/api';\n   115→\n   116→/**\n   117→ * EventBus singleton to publish/subscribe CloudEvents-compliant messages.\n   118→ */\n   119→export class EventBus {\n   120→  static instance;\n   121→\n   122→  /**\n   123→   * Initialise a NATS connection (lazy).\n   124→   * @param {object} config - {servers: string, jwt?: string, nkey?: string}\n   125→   */\n   126→  static async init(config) {\n   127→    if (!EventBus.instance) {\n   128→      EventBus.instance = new EventBus(config);\n   129→      await EventBus.instance._connect();\n   130→    }\n   131→    return EventBus.instance;\n   132→  }\n   133→\n   134→  constructor(config) {\n   135→    this.config = config;\n   136→    this.sc = StringCodec();\n   137→  }\n   138→\n   139→  async _connect() {\n   140→    try {\n   141→      this.nc = await connect(this.config);\n   142→      this.js = this.nc.jetstream();\n   143→      console.info('[EventBus] Connected to NATS');\n   144→    } catch (err) {\n   145→      console.error('[EventBus] Connection error', err);\n   146→      throw err;\n   147→    }\n   148→  }\n   149→\n   150→  /**\n   151→   * Publish a CloudEvent to the given topic.\n   152→   * @param {string} topic - e.g. \"pl.achievement.badge_awarded.v1\"\n   153→   * @param {object} data - event payload\n   154→   * @param {object} [options] - headers, trace context, etc.\n   155→   */\n   156→  async publish(topic, data, options = {}) {\n   157→    if (!this.nc) throw new Error('EventBus not initialised');\n   158→    const span = otel.trace.getTracer('pl-platform').startSpan(`publish:${topic}`);\n   159→    try {\n   160→      const event = {\n   161→        id: uuid(),\n   162→        source: 'pulselearn-campus-hub',\n   163→        specversion: '1.0',\n   164→        type: topic,\n   165→        time: new Date().toISOString(),\n   166→        datacontenttype: 'application/json',\n   167→        data,\n   168→      };\n   169→      // propagate trace context\n   170→      const headers = this._buildHeaders(span, options.headers);\n   171→      await this.js.publish(topic, this.sc.encode(JSON.stringify(event)), { headers });\n   172→      span.setStatus({ code: otel.SpanStatusCode.OK });\n   173→    } catch (err) {\n   174→      span.setStatus({ code: otel.SpanStatusCode.ERROR, message: err.message });\n   175→      throw err;\n   176→    } finally {\n   177→      span.end();\n   178→    }\n   179→  }\n   180→\n   181→  /**\n   182→   * Subscribe to a topic with durable consumer semantics.\n   183→   * @param {string} topic\n   184→   * @param {string} durableName\n   185→   * @param {(evt: object) => Promise<void>} handler\n   186→   */\n   187→  async subscribe(topic, durableName, handler) {\n   188→    const subOpts = { durable: durableName, manualAck: true, ackWait: 60_000 };\n   189→    const sub = await this.js.subscribe(topic, subOpts);\n   190→    console.info(`[EventBus] Subscribed to ${topic}`);\n   191→\n   192→    (async () => {\n   193→      for await (const m of sub) {\n   194→        const span = otel.trace.getTracer('pl-platform').startSpan(`consume:${topic}`);\n   195→        try {\n   196→          const evt = JSON.parse(this.sc.decode(m.data));\n   197→          await handler(evt);\n   198→          m.ack();\n   199→          span.setStatus({ code: otel.SpanStatusCode.OK });\n   200→        } catch (err) {\n   201→          console.error('[EventBus] Handler error', err);\n   202→          // let JetStream redeliver, or add to DLQ via a header\n   203→          span.setStatus({ code: otel.SpanStatusCode.ERROR, message: err.message });\n   204→        } finally {\n   205→          span.end();\n   206→        }\n   207→      }\n   208→    })().catch((err) => console.error('[EventBus] Async iteration error', err));\n   209→  }\n   210→\n   211→  _buildHeaders(span, extra = {}) {\n   212→    const headers = this.nc.headers();\n   213→    const ctx = otel.trace.setSpan(otel.context.active(), span);\n   214→    otel.propagation.inject(ctx, headers, {\n   215→      set: (carrier, key, value) => carrier.set(key, value),\n   216→    });\n   217→    Object.entries(extra).forEach(([k, v]) => headers.set(k, v));\n   218→    return headers;\n   219→  }\n   220→}\n   221→```\n   222→\n   223→### Saga Orchestration Example (Enroll Premium Course)\n   224→\n   225→```javascript\n   226→// services/payment/src/enrollCourseSaga.js\n   227→import { EventBus } from '@pl/platform';\n   228→import { db } from '../db';\n   229→import { generateInvoice } from '../billing';\n   230→import { logger } from '../logger';\n   231→\n   232→export async function registerEventHandlers() {\n   233→  const bus = await EventBus.init({ servers: process.env.NATS_URL });\n   234→\n   235→  // listen to PaymentCaptured\n   236→  await bus.subscribe('pl.payment.captured.v1', 'enroll-premium-course', async (evt) => {\n   237→    const { userId, courseId, paymentId } = evt.data;\n   238→    logger.info('Payment captured', { userId, courseId, paymentId });\n   239→\n   240→    // Step 1: Activate access in course service (synchronous call)\n   241→    try {\n   242→      await activateCourseAccess(userId, courseId);\n   243→    } catch (err) {\n   244→      logger.error('Course activation failed', err);\n   245→      // emit compensating event\n   246→      await bus.publish('pl.payment.refund_requested.v1', { paymentId, reason: 'COURSE_ACTIVATION_FAILED' });\n   247→      return;\n   248→    }\n   249→\n   250→    // Step 2: Emit EnrollmentGranted event\n   251→    await bus.publish('pl.enrollment.granted.v1', { userId, courseId });\n   252→\n   253→    // Step 3: Issue invoice (local DB + external PDF generator)\n   254→    try {\n   255→      const invoiceId = await generateInvoice({ userId, paymentId });\n   256→      await db.invoice.markSent(invoiceId);\n   257→    } catch (err) {\n   258→      logger.error('Invoice generation failed', err);\n   259→      // Non-blocking; instructor & finance will be alerted via DLQ consumer\n   260→    }\n   261→  });\n   262→}\n   263→```\n   264→\n   265→---\n   266→\n   267→## 5. Alternatives Explored (But NOT Chosen)\n   268→\n   269→* **Monolith + Event Module** – insufficient isolation; high coordination cost for multi-squad development.  \n   270→* **Pure Serverless** – latency and unpredictable cost for high-frequency message streams (quizzes, edits).  \n   271→* **gRPC-only Service Mesh** – synchronous coupling failed chaos testing when `AnalyticsService` induced 40 % error rate.\n   272→\n   273→---\n   274→\n   275→## 6. Validation & Next Steps\n   276→\n   277→1. Platform Team to spin up NATS + JetStream in staging (k8s operator).  \n   278→2. Each squad migrates at least one bounded context to the new pattern by Sprint 14.  \n   279→3. Evaluate Confluent vs Redpanda for prod by Q3.  \n   280→4. Conduct load test: 50k concurrent websocket sessions, publish 2k events/s, < 200 ms P99 end-to-end latency.  \n   281→\n   282→---\n   283→\n   284→## 7. References\n   285→\n   286→* “Building Event-Driven Microservices” – O’Reilly, 2021  \n   287→* CloudEvents 1.0 Spec – https://cloudevents.io/  \n   288→* JetStream Acknowledgements – https://docs.nats.io/jetstream/consumers/ack_policy  \n   289→* Saga Pattern – https://microservices.io/patterns/data/saga.html  \n   290→\n   291→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CaaG9mqHk1N9FNA9hVVVRP","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→```markdown\n     2→# ADR-002: Introduce Event Sourcing with Kafka\n     3→\n     4→* Status: **Accepted**  \n     5→* Date: 2024-05-13  \n     6→* Decision Makers: Core Platform Team (PulseLearn Campus Hub)  \n     7→* Supersedes: *N/A*  \n     8→* References: PCH-RFC-11, ADR-001 (Adopt Micro-Service Architecture)  \n     9→\n    10→---\n    11→\n    12→## 1. Context ‑ Why change?\n    13→\n    14→PulseLearn Campus Hub (PCH) is experiencing rapid feature growth:\n    15→\n    16→* Real-time dashboards for quiz progress, peer-review feedback, and badge awards  \n    17→* A new gamification engine that recalculates points on every learning event  \n    18→* Audit-ready reporting for compliance and accreditation bodies  \n    19→\n    20→The existing CRUD-centric model has hit several limits:\n    21→\n    22→| Pain-Point                          | Symptom                                                                             |\n    23→|------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n    24→| Data-coupling                       | Multiple bounded contexts write to the same tables, causing noisy merge conflicts. |\n    25→| Missed notifications                | Lost WebSocket messages because they were dispatched directly from HTTP handlers.   |\n    26→| Operational complexity              | Replaying history (e.g., to debug ranking errors) requires fragile SQL scripts.     |\n    27→| Lack of temporal data               | Hard to answer “What was Alice’s score at 14:01 yesterday?”                         |\n    28→\n    29→To address these, we want:\n    30→\n    31→1. **Immutable event log** – the single source of truth  \n    32→2. **Replayability** – rebuild read-models at any point in time  \n    33→3. **Loose coupling** – services subscribe to what they need, when they need it  \n    34→4. **Scalability** – horizontal partitioning of event streams  \n    35→\n    36→Apache **Kafka** is already used in our data-analytics pipeline; adopting it as the system event backbone is a natural next step.\n    37→\n    38→---\n    39→\n    40→## 2. Decision\n    41→\n    42→We will adopt **event sourcing** for all new domain workflows and gradually migrate existing ones.  \n    43→Kafka will act as the **persistent event store** (commit log) and **message bus**.  \n    44→Specifics:\n    45→\n    46→1. **Each bounded context owns its topic(s)** – e.g. `education.assignment`, `identity.session`, `gamification.badge`.\n    47→2. **Events are immutable** JSON messages, versioned via a `schemaVersion` field; schema registry enforced at the gateway.\n    48→3. **Append-only**: write operations emit events; read models are derived via consumer projections.\n    49→4. **Exactly-once** semantics: achieved by Kafka idempotent producers + transactional writes to outbox tables when necessary.\n    50→5. **Snapshot strategy**: Long-lived aggregates publish `*.snapshot` events every *n* revisions to shorten replay time.\n    51→6. **Backward compatibility**: Services must ignore unknown fields and can only add optional fields to existing event versions.\n    52→\n    53→---\n    54→\n    55→## 3. Consequences\n    56→\n    57→Positive:\n    58→\n    59→* **Auditability** – every state change is traceable.  \n    60→* **Resilience** – consumers can restart and replay without data loss.  \n    61→* **Scalable read models** – each service builds its own query-optimized projection.  \n    62→\n    63→Negative / Trade-offs:\n    64→\n    65→* **Write complexity** – commands must succeed or be compensated by new events.  \n    66→* **Learning curve** – developers must understand eventual consistency.  \n    67→* **Storage cost** – Kafka cluster size grows with retained history.  \n    68→\n    69→---\n    70→\n    71→## 4. Implementation Sketch (JavaScript / Node.js)\n    72→\n    73→Below are cut-down excerpts from production code to guide contributors.  \n    74→All examples assume Node.js ≥ 18, TypeScript, and the `kafkajs` client.\n    75→\n    76→### 4.1 Common Infrastructure\n    77→\n    78→```ts\n    79→// src/infrastructure/kafka/kafkaClient.ts\n    80→import { Kafka, logLevel } from 'kafkajs';\n    81→import config from '../../config';\n    82→\n    83→export const kafka = new Kafka({\n    84→  clientId: 'pulselearn-campus-hub',\n    85→  brokers : config.kafka.brokers,\n    86→  ssl     : true,\n    87→  sasl    : {\n    88→    mechanism : 'scram-sha-256',\n    89→    username  : config.kafka.username,\n    90→    password  : config.kafka.password,\n    91→  },\n    92→  logLevel: logLevel.WARN,\n    93→});\n    94→\n    95→export function topicName(boundedContext: string, suffix = ''): string {\n    96→  return `${config.env}.${boundedContext}${suffix && `.${suffix}`}`;\n    97→}\n    98→```\n    99→\n   100→### 4.2 Event Contracts\n   101→\n   102→```ts\n   103→// src/domain/events/AssignmentSubmittedEvent.ts\n   104→import { z } from 'zod';\n   105→\n   106→export const AssignmentSubmittedEventSchema = z.object({\n   107→  schemaVersion  : z.literal(1),\n   108→  eventId        : z.string().uuid(),\n   109→  aggregateId    : z.string().uuid(),   // Assignment ID\n   110→  type           : z.literal('AssignmentSubmitted'),\n   111→  occurredAt     : z.string().datetime({ offset: true }),\n   112→  payload        : z.object({\n   113→    courseId  : z.string().uuid(),\n   114→    studentId : z.string().uuid(),\n   115→    fileUrl   : z.string().url(),\n   116→  }),\n   117→});\n   118→\n   119→export type AssignmentSubmittedEvent = z.infer<typeof AssignmentSubmittedEventSchema>;\n   120→```\n   121→\n   122→### 4.3 Producer (Command Handler -> Event)\n   123→\n   124→```ts\n   125→// src/application/commands/submitAssignment.ts\n   126→import { AssignmentSubmittedEvent } from '../../domain/events/AssignmentSubmittedEvent';\n   127→import { kafka, topicName } from '../../infrastructure/kafka/kafkaClient';\n   128→import { randomUUID } from 'crypto';\n   129→\n   130→export async function submitAssignment(cmd: {\n   131→  courseId: string;\n   132→  studentId: string;\n   133→  fileUrl: string;\n   134→}): Promise<void> {\n   135→  // Business validation omitted for brevity\n   136→\n   137→  const event: AssignmentSubmittedEvent = {\n   138→    schemaVersion : 1,\n   139→    eventId       : randomUUID(),\n   140→    aggregateId   : randomUUID(),\n   141→    type          : 'AssignmentSubmitted',\n   142→    occurredAt    : new Date().toISOString(),\n   143→    payload       : cmd,\n   144→  };\n   145→\n   146→  const producer = kafka.producer({ idempotent: true });\n   147→  await producer.connect();\n   148→\n   149→  try {\n   150→    await producer.send({\n   151→      topic: topicName('education.assignment'),\n   152→      messages: [\n   153→        {\n   154→          key  : event.aggregateId,\n   155→          value: JSON.stringify(event),\n   156→          headers: {\n   157→            'schema-version': '1',\n   158→            'event-type'    : event.type,\n   159→          },\n   160→        },\n   161→      ],\n   162→    });\n   163→  } finally {\n   164→    await producer.disconnect();\n   165→  }\n   166→}\n   167→```\n   168→\n   169→### 4.4 Consumer Projection (Gamification Points)\n   170→\n   171→```ts\n   172→// src/projections/gamification/pointsProjector.ts\n   173→import { kafka, topicName } from '../../infrastructure/kafka/kafkaClient';\n   174→import { AssignmentSubmittedEventSchema } from '../../domain/events/AssignmentSubmittedEvent';\n   175→import pointsRepo from '../../repositories/pointsRepository';\n   176→\n   177→export async function runPointsProjector(): Promise<void> {\n   178→  const consumer = kafka.consumer({\n   179→    groupId: 'gamification-points-projector-v1',\n   180→  });\n   181→\n   182→  await consumer.connect();\n   183→  await consumer.subscribe({\n   184→    topic: topicName('education.assignment'),\n   185→    fromBeginning: false,\n   186→  });\n   187→\n   188→  await consumer.run({\n   189→    eachMessage: async ({ message, partition }) => {\n   190→      try {\n   191→        const raw = message.value?.toString() ?? '{}';\n   192→        const parsed = AssignmentSubmittedEventSchema.parse(JSON.parse(raw));\n   193→\n   194→        await pointsRepo.increment(\n   195→          parsed.payload.studentId,\n   196→          /* points = */ 5,\n   197→        );\n   198→\n   199→        console.info(\n   200→          `[PointProjector] +5 for student=${parsed.payload.studentId} (partition=${partition})`,\n   201→        );\n   202→      } catch (err) {\n   203→        // DLQ or retry logic could be implemented here\n   204→        console.error('[PointProjector] Failed to process message', err);\n   205→      }\n   206→    },\n   207→  });\n   208→}\n   209→```\n   210→\n   211→### 4.5 Snapshotter Utility\n   212→\n   213→```ts\n   214→// src/utils/snapshotter.ts\n   215→import { kafka, topicName } from '../infrastructure/kafka/kafkaClient';\n   216→\n   217→export async function publishSnapshot<T>({\n   218→  boundedContext,\n   219→  aggregateName,\n   220→  aggregateId,\n   221→  revision,\n   222→  state,\n   223→}: {\n   224→  boundedContext: string;\n   225→  aggregateName: string;\n   226→  aggregateId: string;\n   227→  revision: number;\n   228→  state: T;\n   229→}): Promise<void> {\n   230→  const producer = kafka.producer();\n   231→\n   232→  await producer.connect();\n   233→  try {\n   234→    await producer.send({\n   235→      topic: topicName(`${boundedContext}.${aggregateName}.snapshot`),\n   236→      messages: [\n   237→        {\n   238→          key  : aggregateId,\n   239→          value: JSON.stringify({\n   240→            schemaVersion: 1,\n   241→            type         : `${aggregateName}.snapshot`,\n   242→            revision,\n   243→            occurredAt   : new Date().toISOString(),\n   244→            state,\n   245→          }),\n   246→        },\n   247→      ],\n   248→    });\n   249→  } finally {\n   250→    await producer.disconnect();\n   251→  }\n   252→}\n   253→```\n   254→\n   255→---\n   256→\n   257→## 5. Migration Path\n   258→\n   259→1. **Brown-field integration**: legacy CRUD modules will emit events in a “change-data-capture” style, then slowly migrate command handling logic.  \n   260→2. **Feature toggles**: Read models behind `/v2/` API routes will use event projections; `/v1/` remains on relational reads.  \n   261→3. **Runbook & SRE**: New runbooks added for Kafka cluster monitoring (Lag, ISR, Disk usage).  \n   262→4. **Developer enablement**: Internal workshop + “Event Sourcing 101” wiki page.  \n   263→\n   264→---\n   265→\n   266→## 6. Rejected Alternatives\n   267→\n   268→* **RabbitMQ + Outbox pattern** – flexible routing but lacks strong log-compaction and replay features.  \n   269→* **Full CQRS without Event Sourcing** – improves reads but doesn’t solve auditability or temporal requirements.  \n   270→\n   271→---\n   272→\n   273→## 7. Open Issues / Next Steps\n   274→\n   275→1. Define retention policy per topic (regulatory requirement: 5-year minimum).  \n   276→2. Agree on company-wide naming convention for events (`<Domain>.<Action><PastTense>`).  \n   277→3. Evaluate Confluent Schema Registry vs. open-source alternatives.  \n   278→4. Prototype snapshotting performance with 1M-event aggregate.  \n   279→\n   280→---\n   281→\n   282→> “If you treat data as immutable facts, your systems become a chronicle of truth, not just a reflection of the present.” –  Tech Lead, PCH\n   283→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Jux2HSRSNWfAvmhjSnGNka","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→<!--\n     2→  ADR 003: Polyglot Persistence\n     3→  PulseLearn Campus Hub\n     4→  Status: Accepted\n     5→  Date: 2024-05-28\n     6→-->\n     7→\n     8→# 003 – Polyglot Persistence  \n     9→\n    10→## Status  \n    11→Accepted – The decision has been implemented in `v1.4.0`.\n    12→\n    13→## Context  \n    14→PulseLearn Campus Hub is an event-driven system with a variety of read/write patterns:\n    15→\n    16→* Write-intensive, append-only domain events (≈8 k eps peak)  \n    17→* Read-heavy social timelines that must fan-out to thousands of users in < 150 ms  \n    18→* Strongly-consistent, relational data for billing, LMS compliance, and audit logs  \n    19→* Full-text search over course content and peer-review comments  \n    20→* Graph-style traversals for cohort relationships and content recommendations\n    21→\n    22→A single storage engine cannot optimize for all these needs without unacceptable trade-offs in performance, cost, or developer productivity. We therefore evaluated several strategies:\n    23→\n    24→| Option | Pros | Cons |\n    25→| ------ | ---- | ---- |\n    26→| 1. Single SQL RDBMS (PostgreSQL) | ACID, mature tooling | Hot partitions, expensive scaling, poor search |\n    27→| 2. Single document DB (MongoDB) | Flexible schema, good read scaling | Event ordering issues, multi-doc transactions slower |\n    28→| 3. Polyglot persistence *(selected)* | Fit-for-purpose stores, cost-effective | Operational complexity, cross-store transactions |\n    29→\n    30→## Decision  \n    31→We adopt **polyglot persistence**: each bounded context chooses the persistence technology that best serves its needs, within guardrails enforced by the Platform Team.\n    32→\n    33→1. **Event Store**  \n    34→   *Technology:* Apache Kafka (log), TimescaleDB (cold storage)  \n    35→   *Rationale:* Immutability, temporal queries, built-in replication.\n    36→\n    37→2. **User & Billing**  \n    38→   *Technology:* PostgreSQL 15 with logical replication  \n    39→   *Rationale:* Strong consistency for payments, GDPR data-subject requests.\n    40→\n    41→3. **Realtime Timelines & Notifications**  \n    42→   *Technology:* Redis Cluster + Redis Streams  \n    43→   *Rationale:* Sub-millisecond fan-out, pub/sub semantics, TTL-based eviction.\n    44→\n    45→4. **Search & Analytics**  \n    46→   *Technology:* OpenSearch 2.x  \n    47→   *Rationale:* Relevance scoring, aggregations, horizontal scaling.\n    48→\n    49→5. **Graph Recommendations**  \n    50→   *Technology:* Neo4j 5.x  \n    51→   *Rationale:* Native graph traversals for “students who liked X also liked Y”.\n    52→\n    53→6. **File Storage (Assignments, Media)**  \n    54→   *Technology:* S3-compatible object store with signed URLs  \n    55→   *Rationale:* Durable, CDN-friendly, off-loads traffic from app servers.\n    56→\n    57→### Access Pattern Enforcement  \n    58→```mermaid\n    59→graph TD\n    60→  subgraph Service Layer\n    61→    SVC[Micro-service]\n    62→  end\n    63→  subgraph Repositories\n    64→    REPO1[(PostgreSQL Repo)]\n    65→    REPO2[(Redis Repo)]\n    66→    REPO3[(Kafka Repo)]\n    67→  end\n    68→  SVC --> REPO1 & REPO2 & REPO3\n    69→```\n    70→\n    71→Repositories shield business logic from vendor SDKs. All repositories implement:\n    72→\n    73→```ts\n    74→// docs/examples/Repository.ts\n    75→export interface Repository<T> {\n    76→  findById(id: string): Promise<T | null>;\n    77→  save(entity: T): Promise<void>;\n    78→  delete(id: string): Promise<void>;\n    79→}\n    80→```\n    81→\n    82→Concrete adapters (e.g., `UserPgRepository`, `TimelineRedisRepository`) live in their respective packages.\n    83→\n    84→### Data-Contracts & Async Sync  \n    85→Cross-store ownership is disallowed; instead we replicate immutable projections using Kafka topics suffixed with `.projection`. Consumers materialize local read models, guaranteeing eventual consistency while avoiding distributed transactions.\n    86→\n    87→## Consequences  \n    88→Positive\n    89→* Services scale independently; no single datastore becomes a bottleneck.\n    90→* Teams can evolve schemas without cross-team migration battles.\n    91→* Disaster recovery is narrowed to datastore-specific SLAs.\n    92→\n    93→Negative\n    94→* Ops: multiple backup/restore, monitoring, and upgrade procedures.\n    95→* Observability: needing distributed tracing across store boundaries.\n    96→* Onboarding: developers must learn ≥ 3 query DSLs.\n    97→\n    98→Mitigations\n    99→* Unified secrets via Vault; IaC modules standardize provisioning.\n   100→* Cross-store dashboards in Grafana/Loki/Tempo.\n   101→* Internal workshops and cheat-sheets for new engineers.\n   102→\n   103→## Related Decisions  \n   104→* ADR-001 – Event-Driven Architecture  \n   105→* ADR-002 – Domain-Driven Repository Pattern\n   106→\n   107→## Notes  \n   108→This ADR will be revisited after the next academic year or when datastore OPEX exceeds 20 % of infrastructure budget.\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.\n</system-reminder>\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01Em6KK4Ewbrk5geN3UNJ2f1","session_id":"d9becd8e-6c54-4142-a57f-86d6a46bf7e3","uuid":"21acae8b-7e8f-4ba7-b3d6-bdd8ce60bb1c"}

  3→\n     4→The **Auth Service** is a standalone micro-service that handles:\n     5→\n     6→* Secure user registration & login (local + social OAuth)\n     7→* Stateless JWT authentication with Service.  \n    80→> Each file is self-documented with JSDoc comments & inline explanations.\n    81→\n    82→### 4.1 · `src/config/index.js`\n    83→\n    84→```js\n    85→/**\n    86→ * Application-wide configuration layer.\n    87→ * Loads `.env` variables, validates them, and exports typed config.\n    88→ */\n    89→import 'dotenv/config';\n    90→import Joi from 'joi';\n    91→\n    92→const envSchema = Joi.object({\n    93→  PORT: Joi.number().default(3001),\n    94→  JWT_ACCESS_SECRET: Joi.string().min(32).required(),\n    95→  JWT_REFRESH_SECRET: Joi.string().min(32).required(),\n    96→  JWT_EXPIRES_IN: Joi.string().default('10m'),\n    97→  REFRESH_EXPIRES_IN: Joi.string().default('30d'),\n    98→  BCRYPT_SALT_ROUNDS: Joi.number().default(12),\n    99→  DATABASE_URL: Joi.string().uri().required(),\n   100→  NATS_URL: Joi.string().uri().default('nats://localhost:4222'),\n   101→  LOG_LEVEL: Joi.string().valid('debug', 'info', 'warn', 'error').default('info'),\n   102→}).unknown().required();\n   103→\n   104→const { value: env, error } = envSchema.validate(process.env);\n   105→if (error) {\n   106→  /* eslint-disable no-console */\n   107→  console.error('❌  Invalid environment configuration', error.message);\n   108→  process.exit(1);\n   109→}\n   110→\n   111→export default {\n   112→  port: env.PORT,\n   113→  jwt: {\n   114→    accessSecret: env.JWT_ACCESS_SECRET,\n   115→    refreshSecret: env.JWT_REFRESH_SECRET,\n   116→    accessTTL: env.JWT_EXPIRES_IN,\n   117→    refreshTTL: env.REFRESH_EXPIRES_IN,\n   118→  },\n   119→  bcryptSaltRounds: env.BCRYPT_SALT_ROUNDS,\n   120→  postgresUrl: env.DATABASE_URL,\n   121→  natsUrl: env.NATS_URL,\n   122→  logLevel: env.LOG_LEVEL,\n   123→};\n   124→```\n   125→\n   126→---\n   127→\n   128→### 4.2 · `src/repository/user.repo.js`\n   129→\n   130→```js\n   131→/**\n   132→ * User Repository – data‐access abstraction over PostgreSQL via Prisma ORM.\n   133→ * Encapsulates persistence logic, keeps business layer decoupled from DB.\n   134→ */\n   135→import { PrismaClient } from '@prisma/client';\n   136→import bcrypt from 'bcrypt';\n   137→import config from '../config/index.js';\n   138→\n   139→const prisma = new PrismaClient();\n   140→\n   141→/**\n   142→ * Hashes a plaintext password using bcrypt.\n   143→ * @param {string} password\n   144→ * @returns {Promise<string>} hashed password\n   145→ */\n   146→export const hashPassword = async (password) => {\n   147→  const saltRounds = Number(config.bcryptSaltRounds);\n   148→  return bcrypt.hash(password, saltRounds);\n   149→};\n   150→\n   151→/**\n   152→ * Compares plaintext against hash.\n   153→ * @param {string} password\n   154→ * @param {string} hash\n   155→ * @returns {Promise<boolean>}\n   156→ */\n   157→export const verifyPassword = (password, hash) => bcrypt.compare(password, hash);\n   158→\n   159→export const create = async ({ email, password, role = 'STUDENT' }) => {\n   160→  const hashed = await hashPassword(password);\n   161→  return prisma.user.create({\n   162→    data: {\n   163→      email: email.toLowerCase(),\n   164→      password: hashed,\n   165→      role,\n   166→    },\n   167→  });\n   168→};\n   169→\n   170→export const findByEmail = (email) =>\n   171→  prisma.user.findFirst({ where: { email: email.toLowerCase() } });\n   172→\n   173→export const findById = (id) => prisma.user.findUnique({ where: { id } });\n   174→```\n   175→\n   176→---\n   177→\n   178→### 4.3 · `src/events/publisher.js`\n   179→\n   180→```js\n   181→/**\n   182→ * Simple NATS publisher used by the Auth Service to broadcast domain events.\n   183→ * The rest of the Campus Hub ecosystem consumes these events asynchronously.\n   184→ */\n   185→import { connect, StringCodec } from 'nats';\n   186→import config from '../config/index.js';\n   187→\n   188→const sc = StringCodec();\n   189→\n   190→/**\n   191→ * Connects to NATS and returns a publish function.\n   192→ * @returns {Promise<(subject: string, payload: Record<string,any>) => void>}\n   193→ */\n   194→export const createPublisher = async () => {\n   195→  const nc = await connect({ servers: config.natsUrl });\n   196→  console.log(`🚀  NATS connected (${config.natsUrl})`);\n   197→\n   198→  return async (subject, payload) => {\n   199→    if (nc.isClosed()) {\n   200→      throw new Error('NATS connection closed');\n   201→    }\n   202→    nc.publish(subject, sc.encode(JSON.stringify(payload)));\n   203→  };\n   204→};\n   205→```\n   206→\n   207→---\n   208→\n   209→### 4.4 · `src/middleware/authenticate.js`\n   210→\n   211→```js\n   212→/**\n   213→ * Express middleware that verifies access JWT and injects `req.user`.\n   214→ * On failure, responds with 401 Unauthorized.\n   215→ */\n   216→import jwt from 'jsonwebtoken';\n   217→import config from '../config/index.js';\n   218→\n   219→export const authenticate = (requiredRole) => (req, res, next) => {\n   220→  try {\n   221→    const authHeader = req.headers.authorization || '';\n   222→    const token = authHeader.replace(/^Bearer\\s/, '');\n   223→\n   224→    if (!token) {\n   225→      return res.status(401).json({ message: 'Missing access token' });\n   226→    }\n   227→\n   228→    const payload = jwt.verify(token, config.jwt.accessSecret);\n   229→    // Role-based authorization\n   230→    if (requiredRole && requiredRole !== payload.role) {\n   231→      return res.status(403).json({ message: 'Forbidden' });\n   232→    }\n   233→\n   234→    req.user = payload;\n   235→    return next();\n   236→  } catch (err) {\n   237→    return res.status(401).json({ message: 'Invalid or expired token' });\n   238→  }\n   239→};\n   240→```\n   241→\n   242→---\n   243→\n   244→### 4.5 · `src/routes/auth.routes.js`\n   245→\n   246→```js\n   247→/**\n   248→ * Express router exposing the Auth REST API.\n   249→ * POST /auth/register\n   250→ * POST /auth/login\n   251→ * POST /auth/refresh\n   252→ * POST /auth/logout\n   253→ */\n   254→import { Router } from 'express';\n   255→import jwt from 'jsonwebtoken';\n   256→import {\n   257→  create as createUser,\n   258→  findByEmail,\n   259→  verifyPassword,\n   260→} from '../repository/user.repo.js';\n   261→import config from '../config/index.js';\n   262→import { createPublisher } from '../events/publisher.js';\n   263→\n   264→const router = Router();\n   265→\n   266→let publish; // will be initialized during route registration\n   267→\n   268→const signAccessToken = (user) =>\n   269→  jwt.sign({ id: user.id, role: user.role }, config.jwt.accessSecret, {\n   270→    expiresIn: config.jwt.accessTTL,\n   271→  });\n   272→\n   273→const signRefreshToken = (user) =>\n   274→  jwt.sign({ id: user.id }, config.jwt.refreshSecret, {\n   275→    expiresIn: config.jwt.refreshTTL,\n   276→  });\n   277→\n   278→/**\n   279→ * @route   POST /auth/register\n   280→ */\n   281→router.post('/register', async (req, res) => {\n   282→  try {\n   283→    const { email, password, role } = req.body;\n   284→    if (!email || !password) {\n   285→      return res.status(400).json({ message: 'Missing email or password' });\n   286→    }\n   287→\n   288→    const existing = await findByEmail(email);\n   289→    if (existing) {\n   290→      return res.status(409).json({ message: 'Email already registered' });\n   291→    }\n   292→\n   293→    const user = await createUser({ email, password, role });\n   294→    const accessToken = signAccessToken(user);\n   295→    const refreshToken = signRefreshToken(user);\n   296→\n   297→    await publish('user.registered', {\n   298→      userId: user.id,\n   299→      email: user.email,\n   300→      role: user.role,\n   301→    });\n   302→\n   303→    return res.status(201).json({ accessToken, refreshToken });\n   304→  } catch (err) {\n   305→    return res.status(500).json({ message: err.message });\n   306→  }\n   307→});\n   308→\n   309→/**\n   310→ * @route   POST /auth/login\n   311→ */\n   312→router.post('/login', async (req, res) => {\n   313→  try {\n   314→    const { email, password } = req.body;\n   315→    const user = await findByEmail(email);\n   316→\n   317→    if (!user || !(await verifyPassword(password, user.password))) {\n   318→      return res.status(401).json({ message: 'Invalid credentials' });\n   319→    }\n   320→\n   321→    const accessToken = signAccessToken(user);\n   322→    const refreshToken = signRefreshToken(user);\n   323→\n   324→    await publish('user.logged_in', { userId: user.id });\n   325→\n   326→    return res.json({ accessToken, refreshToken });\n   327→  } catch (err) {\n   328→    return res.status(500).json({ message: err.message });\n   329→  }\n   330→});\n   331→\n   332→/**\n   333→ * @route   POST /auth/refresh\n   334→ */\n   335→router.post('/refresh', (req, res) => {\n   336→  const { token } = req.body;\n   337→  if (!token) {\n   338→    return res.status(400).json({ message: 'Missing refresh token' });\n   339→  }\n   340→\n   341→  try {\n   342→    const payload = jwt.verify(token, config.jwt.refreshSecret);\n   343→    const accessToken = jwt.sign(\n   344→      { id: payload.id },\n   345→      config.jwt.accessSecret,\n   346→      { expiresIn: config.jwt.accessTTL },\n   347→    );\n   348→    return res.json({ accessToken });\n   349→  } catch (err) {\n   350→    return res.status(401).json({ message: 'Invalid refresh token' });\n   351→  }\n   352→});\n   353→\n   354→/**\n   355→ * @route   POST /auth/logout\n   356→ * Body: { allDevices?: boolean }\n   357→ */\n   358→router.post('/logout', (req, res) => {\n   359→  // In stateless JWT flow, logout is handled client-side.\n   360→  // To invalidate all tokens, store an allowlist/denylist in Redis 🡒 not implemented here.\n   361→  publish('session.expired', { userId: req.user?.id, reason: 'logout' }).catch(console.error);\n   362→  return res.status(204).end();\n   363→});\n   364→\n   365→export const initAuthRoutes = async () => {\n   366→  publish = await createPublisher();\n   367→  return router;\n   368→};\n   369→```\n   370→\n   371→---\n   372→\n   373→### 4.6 · `src/server.js`\n   374→\n   375→```js\n   376→/**\n   377→ * Entry point. Bootstraps Express, middleware, routes, and graceful shutdown.\n   378→ */\n   379→import express from 'express';\n   380→import morgan from 'morgan';\n   381→import helmet from 'helmet';\n   382→import cors from 'cors';\n   383→import config from './config/index.js';\n   384→import { initAuthRoutes } from './routes/auth.routes.js';\n   385→\n   386→(async () => {\n   387→  try {\n   388→    const app = express();\n   389→\n   390→    // Global middleware 🔒\n   391→    app.use(helmet());\n   392→    app.use(cors({ origin: '*' }));\n   393→    app.use(express.json());\n   394→    app.use(morgan('dev'));\n   395→\n   396→    // Health check\n   397→    app.get('/healthz', (_, res) =>\n   398→      res.json({ status: 'ok', uptime: process.uptime() }),\n   399→    );\n   400→\n   401→    // Auth routes\n   402→    app.use('/auth', await initAuthRoutes());\n   403→\n   404→    // 404 fallback\n   405→    app.use((_, res) => res.status(404).json({ message: 'Not found' }));\n   406→\n   407→    const server = app.listen(config.port, () => {\n   408→      /* eslint-disable no-console */\n   409→      console.log(`✅  Auth Service listening on :${config.port}`);\n   410→    });\n   411→\n   412→    // Graceful shutdown\n   413→    const shutdown = () => {\n   414→      console.log('📴  Shutting down Auth Service …');\n   415→      server.close(() => process.exit(0));\n   416→    };\n   417→    process.on('SIGINT', shutdown);\n   418→    process.on('SIGTERM', shutdown);\n   419→  } catch (err) {\n   420→    /* eslint-disable no-console */\n   421→    console.error('❌  Failed to start Auth Service', err);\n   422→    process.exit(1);\n   423→  }\n   424→})();\n   425→```\n   426→\n   427→---\n   428→\n   429→## 5 · API Contract (OpenAPI snippet)\n   430→\n Run DB migrations & seed fixtures\n    30→pnpm db:migrate   # => prisma migrate deploy\n    31→pnpm db:seed      # => prisma db seed\n    32→\n    33→# 4. Start the micro-service\n    34→pnpm dev\n    35→```\n    36→\n    37→The service boots on `http://localhost:5001` (REST) and connects to NATS at `nats://localhost:4222` by default.\n    38→\n    39→---\n    40→\n    41→## 🗂️ Folder Structure\n    42→```\n    43→services/\n    44→└─ course-service/\n    45→   ├─ src/\n    46→   │  ├─ api/               # Express routers & OpenAPI docs\n    47→   │  ├─ domain/            # Entities, aggregates, domain logic\n    48→   │  ├─ events/            # NATS publishers/subscribers\n    49→   │  ├─ prisma/            # Prisma schema & migrations\n    50→   │  ├─ services/          # Application services (Service Layer)\n    51→   │  └─ utils/             # Shared helpers & middlewares\n    52→   ├─ tests/                # Jest tests (unit + integration)\n    53→   ├─ Dockerfile\n    54→   ├─ docker-compose.yml\n    55→   ├─ .env.example\n    56→   └─ README.md             # ← You are here\n    57→```\n    58→\n    59→---\n    60→\n    61→## 🛠️ Environment Variables\n    62→\n    63→Copy `.env.example` to `.env` and adjust as needed:\n    64→\n    65→```\n    66→# HTTP\n    67→PORT=5001\n    68→\n    69→# Postgres\n    70→DATABASE_URL=postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:5432/pulselearn_courses\n    71→\n    72→# NATS\n    73→NATS_URL=nats://localhost:4222\n    74→NATS_CLUSTER_ID=pulselearn\n    75→NATS_CLIENT_ID=course-svc-${RANDOM}\n    76→\n    77→# JWT\n    78→JWT_PUBLIC_KEY_PATH=../../certs/jwt_public.pem\n    79→```\n    80→\n    81→⚠️ **Never** commit secrets. 90→### Endpoints\n    91→\n    92→| Method | Path | Description | Auth |\n    93→|--------|------|-------------|------|\n    94→| GET    | `/` | List courses (paginated) | Optional |\n    95→| GET    | `/:courseId` | Get single course | Optional |\n    96→| POST   | `/` | Create new course | Instructor |\n    97→| PATCH  | `/:courseId` | Update course | Instructor (owner) |\n    98→| DELETE | `/:courseId` | Soft-delete course | Admin / Instructor |\n    99→| POST   | `/:courseId/enroll` | Enroll user | Authenticated |\n   100→| DELETE | `/:courseId/enroll/:userId` | Remove enrollment | Instructor / Admin |\n   101→\n   102→Full OpenAPI 3 schema lives under `src/api/openapi.yaml` and is served at `/docs` (Swagger UI).\n   103→\n   104→---\n   105→\n   106→## 🗄️ Data Model (Prisma)\n   107→\n   108→```prisma\n   109→model Course {\n   110→  id          String    @id @default(cuid())\n   111→  title       String\n   112→  description String\n   113→  thumbnail   String?    @db.VarChar(2048)\n   114→  visibility  Visibility @default(PUBLIC)\n   115→  modules     Module[]\n   116→  ownerId     String\n   117→  owner       User       @relation(fields: [ownerId], references: [id])\n   118→  enrollments Enrollment[]\n   119→  createdAt   DateTime   @default(now())\n   120→  updatedAt   DateTime   @updatedAt\n   121→}\n   122→```\n   123→\n   124→See `schema.prisma` for the full ERD.\n   125→\n   126→---\n   127→\n   128→## 📣 Domain Events\n   129→\n   130→| Event | Payload | Published On | Consumers |\n   131→|-------|---------|--------------|-----------|\n   132→| `CourseCreated` | `courseId`, `ownerId` | after successful POST `/` | Achievement Svc, Search Indexer |\n   133→| `CourseDeleted` | `courseId`, `softDelete` | DELETE endpoint | Notification Svc |\n   134→| `EnrollmentAdded` | `courseId`, `userId`, `role` | when a user enrolls | Notification Svc, Gamification Svc |\n   135→| `EnrollmentRemoved` | `courseId`, `userId` | on unenroll | Notification Svc |\n   136→\n   137→All events are JSON-encoded and published over NATS Streaming (JetStream in prod).\n   138→\n   139→---\n   140→\n   141→## 🏗️ Sample Usage (Frontend)\n   142→\n   143→```js\n   144→import axios from 'axios';\n   145→import { getAuthToken } from '@/lib/auth';\n   146→\n   147→export async function createCourse(form) {\n   148→  const { data } = await axios.post(\n   149→    '/api/v1/courses',\n   150→    {\n   151→      title: form.title,\n   152→      description: form.description,\n   153→      thumbnail: form.thumbnailUrl,\n   154→      visibility: 'PRIVATE',\n   155→    },\n   156→    {\n   157→      headers: {\n   158→        Authorization: `Bearer ${getAuthToken()}`,\n   159→      },\n 171→| Script | Description |\n   172→|--------|-------------|\n   173→| `dev` | Run service in watch-mode (ts-node / nodemon) |\n   174→| `build` | Compile TypeScript & bundle for production |\n   175→| `start` | Start compiled JavaScript |\n   176→| `test` | Jest unit + integration tests |\n   177→| `lint` | ESLint + Prettier fix |\n   178→| `docker:up` | Spin up Postgres & NATS via docker-compose |\n   179→| `docker:down` | Stop and remove containers |\n   180→| `db:migrate` | Apply Prisma migrations |\n   181→| `db:seed` | Seed dev data |\n   182→\n   183→---\n   184→\n   185→## ✅ Testing Strategy\n   186→\n   187→1. **Unit Tests** – Pure functions, domain logic – run in CI on every PR.  \n   188→2. **Integration Tests** – Spawns an ephemeral Postgres DB (via `docker-compose -p test`).  \n   189→3. **Contract Tests** – Pact JS ensures consumers (front-end) respect the OpenAPI spec.  \n   190→\n   191→Run all locally:\n   192→\n   193→```bash\n   194→pnpm test           # jest --runInBand\n   195→pnpm test:watch\n   196→```\n   197→\n   198→---\n   199→\n   200→## 🚀 Deployment\n   201→\n   202→* **CI**: GitHub Actions  \n   203→* **CD**: Argo CD deploys Helm chart to Kubernetes  \n   204→* **Observability**: Winston > Logstash > Kibana, Prometheus metrics exposed on `/metrics`  \n   205→\n   206→K8s readiness/liveness probes hit `/healthz` and `/readyz`.  \n   207→\n   208→---\n   209→\n   210→## ⚠️ Gotchas\n   211→\n   212→* Publishing events before DB commit may lead to lost messages. We use the **Transactional Outbox** pattern to guarantee atomicity.\n   213→* Large cohort imports should be processed via the **Bulk Enroll** endpoint (`/api/v1/courses/:id/enroll/bulk`) which streams CSV rows.\n   214→* **Idempotency-Key** header is required on all mutating requests to avoid duplicate transactions.\n   215→\n   216→---\n   217→\n   218→## 🤝 Contributing\n   219→\n   220→1. ARCHITECTURE DECISIONS (ADRs)\n\n### ADR 001 - Microservices Architecture\n**Location:** `/app/project/docs/ADR/001-microservices-architecture.md`\n\n- **Status:** Accepted\n- **Date:** 2024-05-19\n- **Decision:** Event-driven microservices with domain-oriented bounded contexts\n- **Key Points:**\n  - Each service owns its database (Database per Service pattern)\n  - Asynchronous communication via Kafka/NATS\n  - CloudEvents 1.0 spec for event format\n  - Topic naming: `pl.<boundedContext>.<eventName>.<version>`\n  - Shared `@pl/platform` library for event bus abstraction\n  - Saga pattern for distributed transactions\n  - SPIFFE IDs for zero-trust service identity\n  - OpenTelemetry for distributed tracing\n\n### ADR 002 - Event Sourcing with Kafka\n**Location:** `/app/project/docs/ADR/002-event-sourcing-with-kafka.md`\n\n- **Status:** Accepted\n- **Date:** 2024-05-13\n- **Decision:** Kafka as persistent event store and message bus\n- **Key Points:**\n  - Immutable event log as single source of truth\n  - Per-bounded-context topics (e.g., `education.assignment`, `identity.session`)\n  - Schema Registry for versioned schemas\n  - Exactly-once semantics via idempotent producers\n  - Snapshot strategy for long-lived aggregates\n  - Transactional Outbox pattern for atomicity\n  - Event versioning via `schemaVersion` field\n  - Consumer projections for read models\n\n### ADR 003 - Polyglot Persistence\n**Location:** `/app/project/docs/ADR/003-polyglot-persistence.md`\n\n- **Status:** Accepted\n- **Date:** 2024-05-28\n- **Decision:** Use fit-for-purpose datastores within architectural guardrails\n- **Datastore Selection:**\n  1. **Kafka** - Event store and log (immutable, temporal queries)\n  2. **PostgreSQL 15** - User, billing, audit logs (ACID, GDPR)\n  3. **Redis Cluster** - Timelines, notifications, sessions (sub-ms fan-out)\n  4. **OpenSearch 2.x** - Search & analytics (relevance, aggregations)\n  5. **Neo4j 5.x** - Graph recommendations (student relationships)\n  6. **S3/MinIO** - File storage (assignments, media)\n- **Cross-store Sync:** Kafka projection topics for eventual consistency\n- **Pattern:** Repository abstraction layer shields business logic from vendor SDKs\n\n---\n\n## 6. DOMAIN EVENTS & MESSAGE FLOW\n\n### Key Domain Events\n| Event | Payload | Trigger | Consumers |\n|-------|---------|---------|-----------|\n| `CourseCreated` | courseId, ownerId | POST /courses | Achievement Service, Search Indexer |\n| `CoursePublished` | courseId, timestamp | PATCH /courses/:id/publish | Notification Service |\n| `EnrollmentAdded` | courseId, userId, role | User enrolls in course | Notification, Gamification Services |\n| `AssignmentSubmitted` | assignmentId, userId, fileUrl | Student uploads assignment | Gamification Service (points), Badge Service |\n| `BadgeAwarded` | badgeId, userId, reason | Gamification decision | Dashboard updates via WebSocket |\n| `SessionExpired` | userId, expiresAt | JWT TTL expiration | Cleanup services |\n| `LectureUploaded` | lectureId, courseId | Instructor uploads content | Search indexing, CDN delivery |\n| `CoursePaid` | orderId, userId, amount | Stripe webhook | Enrollment activation, Invoice generation |\n\n### Event Flow Pattern\n1. **Producer:** Service executes command, publishes immutable event to Kafka/NATS\n2. **Event Store:** Kafka log compacts and replicates events\n3. **Consumers:** Other services subscribe to topics and project read models\n4. **Idempotency:** Consumers use event IDs to prevent duplicate processing\n5. **DLQ:** Poison messages sent to dead-letter queue for manual inspection\n\n---\n\n## 7. FRONTEND COMPONENT STRUCTURE DETAILS\n\n### DashboardPage Architecture\n**File:** `/app/project/frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.js`\n\n**Key Features:**\n- Real-time metrics dashboard with live updates\n- WebSocket integration via Socket.io\n- Metrics displayed: courses completed, assignments submitted, badges, quiz scores\n- Progress chart using Recharts (weekly learning time)\n- Activity stream with recent user actions\n- Graceful session timeout handling\n- Abort controller for request cancellation\n- Error boundaries and toast notifications\n\n**Data Flow:**\n```\nDashboardPage (mounted)\n  ↓\nDashboardService.getSnapshot() (initial load)\n  ↓\nio(WS_ENDPOINT) connects with JWT auth\n  ↓\nsocket.on('dashboard-event') listens for:\n  - BadgeAwarded → updates badge count\n  - AssignmentSubmitted → updates submission count\n  - ProgressUpdated → refresh chart data\n  - FeedItem → prepend to activity feed\n```\n\n### Component Hierarchy\n- **Pages** (route-level components)\n  - **Layout Components** (Header, Sidebar, Footer)\n    - **Section Components** (dashboard sections, course cards)\n      - **Common Components** (Button, Input, Modal, Spinner)\n\n---\n\n## 8.
