INFRASTRUCTURE & DEPLOYMENT\n\n### Docker Compose Stack (`/app/project/docker-compose.yml`)\n\n**Databases & Storage:**\n- `postgres:15-alpine` - Relational data store (courses, users, billing)\n- `redis:7-alpine` - Caching, sessions, pub/sub\n- `minio` - S3-compatible object storage for files\n- `elasticsearch:8.11.1` - Search indexing\n- `kibana:8.11.1` - Search & observability dashboards\n\n**Message Brokers:**\n- `kafka:7.5.0` + `zookeeper:7.5.0` - Event streaming (Kafka)\n- `nats:2.10-alpine` - JetStream for low-latency messaging\n\n**Supporting Services:**\n- `mailhog:v1.0.1` - Email testing in development\n- `nginx:1.25-alpine` - SSL/TLS reverse proxy\n\n**Application Services:**\n- `api-gateway` - Port 8080\n- `user-service` - Port 7001\n- `activity-service` - Port 7002\n- `notification-service` - Port 7003\n- `recommendation-service` - Port 7004\n- `admin-ui` - Port 3000\n\n**Migrations:**\n- `migrate` - One-shot container for database migrations\n\n### Key Configuration Files\n\n**docker-compose.yml** (`/app/project/docker-compose.yml`)\n- 363 lines\n- Comprehensive production-like local development setup\n- Service health checks and dependencies\n- Environment variable management\n- Volume definitions for persistent data\n- Network isolation (frontend, backend)\n- Network ports exposed for development\n\n**nginx.conf** (`/app/project/services/api-gateway/nginx.conf`)\n- JavaScript-based dynamic configuration builder\n- 268 lines\n- Environment-driven upstream service configuration\n- SSL/TLS configuration\n- Rate limiting setup\n- Security headers (HSTS, CSP, X-Frame-Options)\n- Gzip compression\n- Location-based routing to microservices\n\n**.env.example** (`/app/project/.env.example`)\n- 150+ configuration variables\n- Database credentials\n- JWT secrets\n- Redis URL\n- Kafka/NATS configuration\n- OAuth provider keys\n- Stripe API keys\n- AWS S3 configuration\n- SMTP/Email settings\n- Feature flags\n- Rate limiting parameters\n\n---\n\n## 5. ARCHITECTURE DECISIONS (ADRs)\n\n### ADR 001 - Microservices Architecture\n**Location:** `/app/project/docs/ADR/001-microservices-architecture.md`\n\n- **Status:** Accepted\n- **Date:** 2024-05-19\n- **Decision:** Event-driven microservices with domain-oriented bounded contexts\n- **Key Points:**\n  - Each service owns its database (Database per Service pattern)\n  - Asynchronous communication via Kafka/NATS\n  - CloudEvents 1.0 spec for event format\n  - Topic naming: `pl.<boundedContext>.<eventName>.<version>`\n  - Shared `@pl/platform` library for event bus abstraction\n  - Saga pattern for distributed transactions\n  - SPIFFE IDs for zero-trust service identity\n  - OpenTelemetry for distributed tracing\n\n### ADR 002 - Event Sourcing with Kafka\n**Location:** `/app/project/docs/ADR/002-event-sourcing-with-kafka.md`\n\n- **Status:** Accepted\n- **Date:** 2024-05-13\n- **Decision:** Kafka as persistent event store and message bus\n- **Key Points:**\n  - Immutable event log as single source of truth\n  - Per-bounded-context topics (e.g., `education.assignment`, `identity.session`)\n  - Schema Registry for versioned schemas\n  - Exactly-once semantics via idempotent producers\n  - Snapshot strategy for long-lived aggregates\n  - Transactional Outbox pattern for atomicity\n  - Event versioning via `schemaVersion` field\n  - Consumer projections for read models\n\n### ADR 003 - Polyglot Persistence\n**Location:** `/app/project/docs/ADR/003-polyglot-persistence.md`\n\n- **Status:** Accepted\n- **Date:** 2024-05-28\n- **Decision:** Use fit-for-purpose datastores within architectural guardrails\n- **Datastore Selection:**\n  1. **Kafka** - Event store and log (immutable, temporal queries)\n  2. **PostgreSQL 15** - User, billing, audit logs (ACID, GDPR)\n  3. **Redis Cluster** - Timelines, notifications, sessions (sub-ms fan-out)\n  4. **OpenSearch 2.x** - Search & analytics (relevance, aggregations)\n  5. **Neo4j 5.x** - Graph recommendations (student relationships)\n  6. **S3/MinIO** - File storage (assignments, media)\n- **Cross-store Sync:** Kafka projection topics for eventual consistency\n- **Pattern:** Repository abstraction layer shields business logic from vendor SDKs\n\n---\n\n## 6. DOMAIN EVENTS & MESSAGE FLOW\n\n### Key Domain Events\n| Event | Payload | Trigger | Consumers |\n|-------|---------|---------|-----------|\n| `CourseCreated` | courseId, ownerId | POST /courses | Achievement Service, Search Indexer |\n| `CoursePublished` | courseId, timestamp | PATCH /courses/:id/publish | Notification Service |\n| `EnrollmentAdded` | courseId, userId, role | User enrolls in course | Notification, Gamification Services |\n| `AssignmentSubmitted` | assignmentId, userId, fileUrl | Student uploads assignment | Gamification Service (points), Badge Service |\n| `BadgeAwarded` | badgeId, userId, reason | Gamification decision | Dashboard updates via WebSocket |\n| `SessionExpired` | userId, expiresAt | JWT TTL expiration | Cleanup services |\n| `LectureUploaded` | lectureId, courseId | Instructor uploads content | Search indexing, CDN delivery |\n| `CoursePaid` | orderId, userId, amount | Stripe webhook | Enrollment activation, Invoice generation |\n\n### Event Flow Pattern\n1. **Producer:** Service executes command, publishes immutable event to Kafka/NATS\n2. **Event Store:** Kafka log compacts and replicates events\n3. **Consumers:** Other services subscribe to topics and project read models\n4. **Idempotency:** Consumers use event IDs to prevent duplicate processing\n5. **DLQ:** Poison messages sent to dead-letter queue for manual inspection\n\n---\n\n## 7. FRONTEND COMPONENT STRUCTURE DETAILS\n\n### DashboardPage Architecture\n**File:** `/app/project/frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.js`\n\n**Key Features:**\n- Real-time metrics dashboard with live updates\n- WebSocket integration via Socket.io\n- Metrics displayed: courses completed, assignments submitted, badges, quiz scores\n- Progress chart using Recharts (weekly learning time)\n- Activity stream with recent user actions\n- Graceful session timeout handling\n- Abort controller for request cancellation\n- Error boundaries and toast notifications\n\n**Data Flow:**\n```\nDashboardPage (mounted)\n  ↓\nDashboardService.getSnapshot() (initial load)\n  ↓\nio(WS_ENDPOINT) connects with JWT auth\n  ↓\nsocket.on('dashboard-event') listens for:\n  - BadgeAwarded → updates badge count\n  - AssignmentSubmitted → updates submission count\n  - ProgressUpdated → refresh chart data\n  - FeedItem → prepend to activity feed\n```\n\n### Component Hierarchy\n- **Pages** (route-level components)\n  - **Layout Components** (Header, Sidebar, Footer)\n    - **Section Components** (dashboard sections, course cards)\n      - **Common Components** (Button, Input, Modal, Spinner)\n\n---\n\n## 8. SAVE_INTERVAL_MS = 5_000; // Persist progress every 5 seconds\nconst SEEK_EPSILON = 1; // When comparing times, treat +/- 1 second as equal\n\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Helpers\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n/**\n * Round a */\nconst isNearlyEqual = (a, b, epsilon = SEEK_EPSILON) => Math.abs(a - b) < epsilon;\n\n/**\n * A hook that returns a function throttled to a given interval.\n */\nconst useThrottledCallback = (callback, delay) =>\n  useCallback(throttle(callback, delay, { leading: false, trailing: true }), [\n    callback,\n    delay,\n  ]);\n\n// LecturePlayer = ({\n  courseId,\n  lectureId,\n  src,\n  poster,\n  captionSrc,\n  startAt = 0,\n  autoPlay = false,\n}) => {\n  //    [courseId, lectureId],\n  );\n\n  // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  // API Calls\n  // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  const saveProgress = useCallback(\n    async (seconds) => {\n      try {\n        await api.put(\n          `/courses/${courseId}/lectures/${lectureId}/progress`,\n          { seconds: roundTime(seconds) },\n        );\n      } catch (e) {\n        /* Non-blocking: log and continue */\n        // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  const handleReady = () => {\n    setReady(true);\n    /* Seek to previous checkpoint (if any) */\n    if setBuffering(false);\n\n  const handleEnded = async () => {\n    setPlaying(false);\n    emitEvent('LectureCompleted', { duration: roundTime(progress.playedSeconds) });\n    try {\n      await api.post(`/courses/${courseId}/lectures/${lectureId}/complete`);\n    } catch (e) {\n      // eslint-disable-next-line no-console\n      console.error('Failed marking lecture complete', e);\n    }\n  };\n\n  const handleError = (e) => {\n    setError(e?.message || 'Unknown error');\n    emitEvent('LecturePlaybackError', { message: e?.message });\n  };\n\n  // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  // Fullscreen\n  // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  const toggleFullscreen = () => {\n    if (screenfull.isEnabled && containerRef.current) {\n      screenfull.toggle(containerRef.current);\n    }\n  };\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    if (!screenfull.isEnabled) return;\n\n    const onChange = () => setIsFullscreen(screenfull.isFullscreen);\n    screenfull.on('change', onChange);\n    return () => screenfull.off('change', onChange);\n  }, []);\n\n  // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  // Cleanup on Unmount\n  // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  useEffect(\n    () => () => {\n      throttledSaveProgress.cancel();\n      /* On unmount, persist the latest progress synchronously */\n      saveProgress(progress.playedSeconds);\n      emitEvent('LecturePlayerUnmount', { at: roundTime(progress.playedSeconds) });\n    },\n    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps\n    [],\n  );\n\n  // 'hidden',\n      }}\n    >\n      {/* Video Layer */}\n      <ReactPlayer\n        ref={playerRef}\n        url={src}\n        light={poster}\n        playing={playing}\n        controls={false} // Custom controls\n        width=\"100%\"\n        height=\"100%\"\n        muted={muted}\n        volume={volume}\n        onReady={handleReady}\n        onPlay={() => emitEvent('LecturePlayed')}\n        onPause={() => emitEvent('LecturePaused')}\n        onError={handleError}\n        onProgress={handleProgress}\n        onBuffer={handleBuffer}\n        onBufferEnd={handleBufferEnd}\n        onEnded={handleEnded}\n        config={{\n          file: {\n            attributes: {\n              crossOrigin: 'anonymous',\n              poster,\n            },\n            tracks: captionSrc\n              ? [\n                  {\n                    kind: 'subtitles',\n                    src: captionSrc,\n                    srcLang: 'en',\n                    default: true,\n                  },\n                ]\n              : [],\n          },\n        }}\n      />\n\n      {/* Buffering indicator */}\n      {buffering && (\n        <Box\n          sx={{\n            position: 'absolute',\n            top: 0,\n            left: 0,\n            width: '100%',\n          }}\n        >\n          <LinearProgress color=\"secondary\" />\n        </Box>\n      )}\n\n      {/* Error overlay */}\n      {error && (\n        <Stack\n          spacing={1}\n          alignItems=\"center\"\n          justifyContent=\"center\"\n          sx={{\n            position: 'absolute',\n            inset: 0,\n            backdropFilter: 'blur(3px)',\n            color: 'common.white',\n            textAlign: 'center',\n          }}\n        >\n          <ErrorOutline fontSize=\"large\" />\n          <Typography variant=\"h6\">Playback error</Typography>\n          <Typography variant=\"body2\">{error}</Typography>\n        </Stack>\n      )}\n\n      {/* Control Bar */}\n      <Stack\n        direction=\"row\"\n        alignItems=\"center\"\n        sx={{\n          position: 'absolute',\n          bottom: 0,\n          left: 0,\n          width: '100%',\n          bgcolor: 'rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.6)',\n          px: 1,\n          py: 0.5,\n        }}\n      >\n        {/* Play / Pause */}\n        <Tooltip title={playing ? curr.course);\n              if (!existing || existing.percent < curr.percent) {\n                return [...acc.filter((d) => d.course !== curr.course), curr];\n              }\n              return Reconnecting…');\n        // eslint-disable-next-line no-console\n        console.error('Progress WS error', e);\n      });\n    };\n\n    connect();\n\n    // Clean up on component unmount\n    return () => {\n      forcedClose = true;\n      if (wsRef.current && wsRef.current.readyState === WebSocket.OPEN) {\n        wsRef.current.close();\n      }\n    };\n  }, [userId, mapEventToDatum]);\n\n  return { data, loading, error };\n}\n\n// ---------- Components ------------------------------------------------------\n\n/**\n * ProgressChart\n *\n * @param {{\n *   userId: chartData = data.length ? data : placeholderData;\n\n  const renderContent = () => {\n    if (error) {\n      return (\n        <div\n          style={{\n            padding: 16,\n            color: 'var(--color-danger)',\n            fontSize: 14,\n            textAlign: 'center',\n          }}\n        >\n          {error}\n        </div>\n      );\n    }\n\n    if (loading && !chartData.length) {\n      return (\n        <div\n          style={{\n            padding: 16,\n            fontSize: 14,\n            textAlign: 'center',\n            opacity: 0.7,\n          }}\n fontSize: 14,\n            textAlign: 'center',\n          }}\n        >\n          No activity yet. 🚀\n        </div>\n      );\n    }\n\n    // Limit number of courses to keep chart readable\n Built with accessibility, performance, and resiliency in mind.\n */\n\nimport React, {\n  memo,\n  useCallback,\n  useEffect,\n  useRef,\n  useState,\n} from 'react';\nimport PropTypes from                                                                */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/**\n * Throttles a function so it is executed at most once every `limit` ms.\n */\nconst throttle = (fn, limit = 200) => {\n  let inThrottle;\n  return (...args) => {\n    if (!inThrottle) {\n      // eslint-disable-next-line prefer-spread\n      fn.apply(null, args);\n      inThrottle = true;\n      setTimeout(() => (inThrottle = false), limit);\n    }\n  };\n};\n\n/**\n * Formats a timestamp into a relative string (e.g., \"3 minutes page: pageNumber,\n            size: PAGE_SIZE,\n            userId,\n          },\n          headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` },\n          timeout: 10_000,\n        });\n\n        const { items, last } = response.data;\n\n        setActivities((prev) =>\n          // De-duplicate by id so websocket items don't double-insert\n          [\n            ...prev,\n            ...items.filter((it) => !prev.some((p) => p.id === it.id)),\n          ].sort((a, b) => new Date(b.timestamp) - new Date(a.timestamp)),\n        );\n        setHasMore(!last);\n        setPage(pageNumber);\n      } catch (err) {\n        setError(\n          err.response?.data?.message ||\n            err.message ||\n            'Failed to load activity stream.',\n        );\n        toast.error('Unable to load activity stream. scrollHeight, clientHeight } = feedRef.current;\n\n      // If scrolled within 100px from bottom, load more\n      if (scrollHeight - scrollTop - clientHeight < 100) {\n        setLoadingMore(true);\n };\n\n    socket.onclose = (e) => {\n      // eslint-disable-next-line no-console\n      console.warn('[ActivityStream] WebSocket closed', e.reason);\n    };\n\n    return () => socket.close(1000, Uses Redis with JSON serialization.\n * @param {String} key\n * @param {Function} resolver - Function that returns a Promise of the data\n * @param {Number} ttlSeconds - Time-to-live\n */\nasync function getOrSetCache(key, resolver, ttlSeconds = 60) {\n  const cached = await redis.get(key);\n  if (cached) {\n    return JSON.parse(cached);\n  }\n  const fresh = await resolver();\n  await redis.set(key, JSON.stringify(fresh), 'EX', ttlSeconds);\n  return fresh;\n}\n\n/* ───────────────────────────────── Domain Service ────────────────────────── */\nclass CourseService {\n  /* ======================================================================= *\n   * PUBLIC API                                                              *\n   * ======================================================================= */\n\n  /**\n   * Creates a brand-new course and its initial metadata.\n   * @param {Object} courseData\n   * @param {String} creatorId\n   * @returns {Promise<Object>} Newly created course\n   */\n  async createCourse(courseData, creatorId) {\n    const { title, description, category, thumbnailUrl, publishedAt } = courseData;\n\n    // Basic validation\n    if (!title || title.length < 3) {\n      throw new AppError('Course title must be at least 3 characters long', ErrorCodes.VALIDATION);\n    }\n\n    try {\n      const course = await prisma.$transaction(async (tx) => {\n        // 1. Insert into Course table\n        const newCourse = await tx.course.create({\n          data: {\n            id          : uuid(),\n            title,\n            description,\n            category,\n            thumbnailUrl,\n            publishedAt : publishedAt || null,\n            creatorId,\n            status      : 'DRAFT'\n          }\n        });\n\n        // 2. Auto-enroll creator as instructor\n        await tx.courseEnrollment.create({\n          data: {\n            userId  : creatorId,\n            courseId: newCourse.id,\n            role    : 'INSTRUCTOR'\n          }\n        });\n\n        return newCourse;\n      });\n\n      // 3. Publish event outside transaction\n      await publishEvent(EventTopics.COURSE_CREATED, {\n        courseId : course.id,\n        creatorId,\n        timestamp: new Date().toISOString()\n      });\n\n      return course;\n    } catch (err) {\n      logger.error('createCourse failed', { err: err.stack || err });\n      if (err instanceof Prisma.PrismaClientKnownRequestError && err.code === 'P2002') {\n        // Unique constraint.\n        throw new AppError('A course with the same title already exists', ErrorCodes.CONFLICT);\n      }\n      throw err;\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Retrieves a course by its ID, with optional lessons & enrollment info.\n   * Makes heavy use of Redis for fast repeated look-ups.\n   *\n   * @param {String} courseId\n   * @param {Object} [options]\n   * @param {Boolean} [options.includeLessons=false]\n   * @param {Boolean} [options.includeStats=false]\n   */\n  async getCourseById(courseId, options = {}) {\n    if (!courseId) throw new AppError('courseId is required', ErrorCodes.VALIDATION);\n    const { includeLessons = false, includeStats = false } = options;\n\n    const cacheKey = `course:${courseId}:${includeLessons}:${includeStats}`;\n\n    return getOrSetCache(\n      cacheKey,\n      async () => {\n        const course = await prisma.course.findUnique({\n          where: { id: courseId },\n          include: {\n            lessons: includeLessons,\n            // stats is a materialized view; simulated via compute\n          }\n        });\n\n        if (!course) {\n          throw new AppError('Course not found', ErrorCodes.NOT_FOUND);\n        }\n\n        if (includeStats) {\n          const [enrolled, completed] = await Promise.all([\n            prisma.courseEnrollment.count({ where: { courseId } }),\n            prisma.lessonCompletion.count({ where: { courseId } })\n          ]);\n          course.stats = { enrolled, completed };\n        }\n\n        return course;\n      },\n      30 // seconds\n    );\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Updates mutable fields of a course.  Immutable fields like id and creatorId\n   * are ignored automatically via lodash.pick.\n   *\n   * @param {String} courseId\n   * @param {Object} updates\n   * @param {String} requesterId - Authorization performed here.\n   */\n  async updateCourse(courseId, updates, requesterId) {\n    const allowed = ['title', 'description', 'category', 'thumbnailUrl', 'publishedAt', 'status'];\n    const sanitized = _.pick(updates, allowed);\n\n    // Early bail-out\n    if (_.isEmpty(sanitized)) {\n      throw new AppError('No valid fields to update', ErrorCodes.VALIDATION);\n    }\n\n    // Authorization: only creator or admin can update\n    const course = await prisma.course.findUnique({ where: { id: courseId } });\n    if (!course) throw new AppError('Course not found', ErrorCodes.NOT_FOUND);\n\n    const isAdmin = await this.#isAdmin(requesterId);\n    if (course.creatorId !== requesterId && !isAdmin) {\n      throw new AppError('Forbidden', ErrorCodes.FORBIDDEN, 403);\n    }\n\n    try {\n      const updated = await prisma.course.update({\n        where: { id: courseId },\n        data : sanitized\n      });\n\n      // Invalidate cache\n      await redis.del(`course:${courseId}:*`);\n\n      await publishEvent(EventTopics.COURSE_UPDATED, {\n        courseId,\n        updaterId: requesterId,\n        changes  : Object.keys(sanitized),\n        timestamp: new Date().toISOString()\n      });\n\n      return updated;\n    } catch (err) {\n      logger.error('updateCourse failed', { err: err.stack || err });\n      throw err;\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Enroll a user into a course, supports roles: STUDENT, AUDITOR\n   *\n   * @param {String} courseId\n   * @param {String} userId\n   * @param {\"STUDENT\"|\"AUDITOR\"} role\n   */\n  async enrollUser(courseId, userId, role = 'STUDENT') {\n    if (!['STUDENT', 'AUDITOR'].includes(role)) {\n      throw new AppError('Invalid role', ErrorCodes.VALIDATION);\n    }\n\n    try {\n      await prisma.courseEnrollment.create({\n        data: {\n          id      : uuid(),\n          courseId,\n          userId,\n          role\n        }\n      });\n\n      // Bump course enrollment count // Unique constraint violation → already enrolled\n        throw new AppError('User already enrolled', ErrorCodes.CONFLICT);\n      }\n      throw err;\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Tracks lesson completion for a learner.\n   * @param {String} courseId\n   * @param {String} lessonId\n   * @param {String} userId\n   */\n  async completeLesson(courseId, lessonId, userId) {\n    const idempotencyKey = `${userId}:${lessonId}:completion`;\n    const lock = await redis.set(idempotencyKey, '1', 'NX', 'EX', 5);\n    if (!lock) {\n      // Duplicate submission within 5 seconds\n      return;\n    }\n\n    try {\n      await prisma.lessonCompletion.create({\n        data: {\n          id       : uuid(),\n          courseId,\n          lessonId,\n          userId\n        }\n      });\n\n      await publishEvent(EventTopics.LESSON_COMPLETED, {\n        courseId,\n        lessonId,\n        userId,\n        timestamp: new Date().toISOString()\n      });\n\n      await redis.del(`course:${courseId}:*`);\n    } catch (err) {\n      if (err instanceof Prisma.PrismaClientKnownRequestError && err.code === 'P2002') {\n        // Duplicate completion (idempotent)\n        return;\n      }\n      throw err;\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Deletes a course; only admins can perform hard-deletes.\n   *\n   * @param {String} courseId\n   * @param {String} requesterId\n   */\n  async deleteCourse(courseId, requesterId) {\n    const isAdmin = await this.#isAdmin(requesterId);\n    if (!isAdmin) {\n      throw new AppError('Admin rights required', ErrorCodes.FORBIDDEN, 403);\n    }\n\n    await prisma.$transaction([\n      prisma.courseEnrollment.deleteMany({ where: { courseId } }),\n      prisma.lessonCompletion.deleteMany({ where: { courseId } }),\n requesterId,\n      timestamp : new Date().toISOString()\n    });\n\n    return { success: true };\n  }\n\n  /* ======================================================================= *\n   * PRIVATE HELPERS                                                         *\n   * ======================================================================= */\n\n  /**\n   * Determines if the user is an admin by checking cached claims.\n   * @private\n   * @param {String} userId\n   * @returns {Promise<Boolean>}\n   */\n  async #isAdmin(userId) {\n    const cacheKey = `user:${userId}:isAdmin`;\n    const cached   = await redis.get(cacheKey);\n    if (cached !== null) return cached === 'true';\n\n    // This would normally hit the Auth/User service. Hard-coded for brevity.\n    const isAdmin = await prisma.user.findUnique({\n      where : { id: userId },\n      select: { roles: true }\n    }).then(u => u?.roles.includes('ADMIN') || false);\n\n    await redis.set(cacheKey, isAdmin, 'EX', 60);\n    return isAdmin;\n  }\n}\n\n/* ───────────────────────── Exports (Singleton by Convention) ─────────────── */\nmodule.exports = new CourseService();\n```","numLines":409,"startLine":1,"totalLines":409}}}

production we could delegate to a proper JSON-schema validator\n * such as Ajv; here we add a minimal safeguard so we do not publish\n * garbage messages onto the bus.\n */\nfunction (!payload.title || typeof payload.title !== 'string') {\n    throw new Error('Invalid payload: \"title\" is required and must be a string');\n  }\n}\n\n// ---- Producer Implementation ----------------------------------------------\n\nclass EventProducer {\n\n  constructor() {\n    this._stan   = null;\n    this._ready  = false;\n    this._queue  = [];\n    this._drainInProgress = false;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Lazily create a connection to the NATS Streaming cluster.\n   * Connection is initialised only once per process.\n   * @returns {Promise<void>}\n   */\n  async _connect() {\n    if (this._stan || this._ready) return;\n    LOGGER.info({ cluster: NATS_CLUSTER_ID, client: NATS_CLIENT_ID, url: NATS_URL },\n                'Connecting to NATS Streaming…');\n\n    this._stan = stan.connect(NATS_CLUSTER_ID, NATS_CLIENT_ID, { url: NATS_URL });\n\n    // Promisify connection lifecycle\n    await new Promise((resolve, reject) => {\n      const onConnect = () => {\n        this._ready = true;\n        LOGGER.info('NATS Streaming connection established ✅');\n        this._flushQueue(); // emit messages that were queued before ready\n        resolve();\n      };\n\n      const onError = (err) => {\n        LOGGER.error({ err }, 'Unable to connect to NATS Streaming ❌');\n        reject(err);\n      };\n\n      this._stan.once('connect', onConnect);\n      this._stan.once('error',   onError);\n    });\n\n    // Attach common listeners AFTER the initial promise resolution\n    this._stan.on('close',  () => LOGGER.warn('NATS connection closed'));\n    this._stan.on('reconnect', () => LOGGER.info('NATS reconnected'));\n    this._stan.on('disconnect', () => LOGGER.warn('NATS disconnected'));\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Drain NATS connection gracefully before process exit.\n   */\n  async close() {\n    if (!this._stan || this._drainInProgress) return;\n    this._drainInProgress = true;\n\n    LOGGER.info('Draining NATS Streaming connection before shutdown…');\n    await new Promise((resolve) => {\n      this._stan.drain(() => {\n        LOGGER.info('NATS drain complete, closing connection');\n        this._stan.close();\n        resolve();\n      });\n    });\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Publish raw subject + message\n   * @param {String} subject\n   * @param {String} message   – already JSON serialised\n   * @param {Object} options   – optional metadata (e.g. persistentName)\n   * @returns {Promise<void>}\n   */\n  async _publish(subject, message, options = {}) {\n    await this._connect();\n\n    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {\n      const guid = this._stan.publish(subject, message, (err, ackGuid) => {\n        if (err) {\n          LOGGER.error({ err, subject, guid }, 'Failed to publish message');\n          return reject(err);\n        }\n        LOGGER.debug({ subject, guid: ackGuid }, 'Event published');\n        resolve();\n      });\n\n      // Fallback: if no ack within ACK_WAIT_MILLIS, reject\n      setTimeout(() => {\n        reject(new Error(`ACK timeout after ${ACK_WAIT_MILLIS}ms for guid: ${guid}`));\n      }, ACK_WAIT_MILLIS).unref();\n    });\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Queue messages if connection hasn't been established yet.\n   * This prevents any writes being lost during cold starts.\n   */\n  _queueMessage(subject, message) {\n    this._queue.push({ subject, message });\n  }\n\n  _flushQueue() {\n    if (!this._queue.length || !this._ready) return;\n\n    LOGGER.info(`Flushing ${this._queue.length} queued event(s)`);\n    this._queue.forEach(({ subject, message }) => {\n      this._stan.publish(subject, message, (err) => {\n        if (err) {\n          LOGGER.error({ subject, err }, 'Failed to publish queued event');\n        }\n      });\n    });\n    this._queue.length = 0;\n  }\n\n  // ---------------------------------------------------\n  // Domain-specific API\n  // ---------------------------------------------------\n\n  /**\n   * Course Created domain event publisher.\n   * @param {Object} course – plain JS object { id, title, … }\n   */\n  async courseCreated(course) {\n    try {\n      validateCoursePayload(course);\n      const msg = buildMessage('CourseCreated', course);\n      await this._publish(SUBJECTS.COURSE_CREATED, msg);\n    } catch (err) {\n      LOGGER.error({ err, course }, 'Unable to publish CourseCreated event');\n      throw err;\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Course Updated domain event publisher.\n   * @param {Object} course – plain JS object { id, title, … }\n   */\n  async courseUpdated(course) {\n    try {\n      validateCoursePayload(course);\n      const msg = buildMessage('CourseUpdated', course);\n      await this._publish(SUBJECTS.COURSE_UPDATED, msg);\n    } catch (err) {\n      LOGGER.error({ err, course }, 'Unable to publish CourseUpdated event');\n      throw err;\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Course archived event publisher.\n   * @param {Object} course – minimal object { id, archivedAt }\n   */\n  async courseArchived(course) {\n    if (!course.id) {\n      throw new Error('Invalid payload: \"id\" is required for archive event');\n    }\n\n    const msg = buildMessage('CourseArchived', course);\n    try {\n      await this._publish(SUBJECTS.COURSE_ARCHIVED, msg);\n    } catch (err) {\n      LOGGER.error({ err, course }, 'Unable to publish CourseArchived event');\n      throw err;\n terminated\n['SIGINT', 'SIGTERM', 'SIGQUIT'].forEach((signal) => {\n  process.on(signal, async () => {\n    LOGGER.info({ signal }, 'Received termination signal');\n    await producerInstance.close();\n    process.exit(0);\n  });\n});\n\nmodule.exports = producerInstance;\n```","numLines":266,"startLine":1,"totalLines":266}}}

transaction }\n    );\n\n    // Bust course cache because nested relation changed\n    if (this.cache) {\n      await this.cache.del(`course:${courseId}`);\n    }\n\n    this.log.info({ courseId, @param {import('sequelize').Transaction} [transaction]\n   * @returns {Promise<object>}\n   */\n  async enrollStudent(courseId, studentId, transaction) {\n    // Idempotency: ensure unique composite index on (courseId, studentId)\n    const [enrollment] = await this.Enrollment.findOrCreate({\n      where: { courseId, studentId },\n      defaults: {\n        id: uuidv4(),\n        courseId,\n        studentId\n      },\n      transaction\n    });\n\n    if (this.cache) {\n      await this.cache.del(`course:${courseId}`);\n    }\n\n    this.log.info({ courseId, studentId }, 'Student enrolled');\n    return enrollment.toJSON();\n  }\n\n  /* ---------------------------------------------------------------------- *\\\n     INTERNAL HELPERS\n  \\* ---------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n  /**\n   * Clears all cached courses.  \n   * Heavy-handed, but useful for admin-level destructive operations.\n   *\n   * @returns {Promise<void>}\n   */\n  async _flushCache() {\n    if (!this.cache) return;\n    const keys = await this.cache.keys('course:*');\n    if (keys.length) await this.cache.del(keys);\n  }\n}\n\nmodule.exports = CourseRepository;\n```","numLines":291,"startLine":1,"totalLines":291}}}

Context ‑ Why change?\n    13→\n    14→PulseLearn Campus Hub (PCH) is experiencing rapid feature growth:\n    15→\n    16→* Real-time dashboards for quiz progress, peer-review feedback, and badge awards  \n    17→* A new gamification engine that recalculates points on every learning event  \n    18→* Audit-ready reporting for compliance and accreditation bodies  \n    19→\n    20→The existing CRUD-centric model has hit several limits:\n    21→\n    22→| Pain-Point                          | Symptom                                                                             |\n    23→|------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n    24→| Data-coupling                       | Multiple bounded contexts write to the same tables, causing noisy merge conflicts. |\n    25→| Missed notifications                | Lost WebSocket messages because they were dispatched directly from HTTP handlers.   |\n    26→| Operational complexity              | Replaying history (e.g., to debug ranking errors) requires fragile SQL scripts.     |\n    27→| Lack of temporal data               | Hard to answer “What was Alice’s score at 14:01 yesterday?”                         |\n    28→\n    29→To address these, we want:\n    30→\n    31→1. **Immutable event log** – the single source of truth  \n    32→2. **Replayability** – rebuild read-models at any point in time  \n    33→3. **Loose coupling** – services subscribe to what they need, when they need it  \n    34→4. **Scalability** – horizontal partitioning of event streams  \n    35→\n    36→Apache **Kafka** is already used in our data-analytics pipeline; adopting it as the system event backbone is a natural next step.\n    37→\n    38→---\n    39→\n    40→## 2. Decision\n    41→\n    42→We will adopt **event sourcing** for all new domain workflows and gradually migrate existing ones.  \n    43→Kafka will act as the **persistent event store** (commit log) and **message bus**.  \n    44→Specifics:\n    45→\n    46→1. **Each bounded context owns its topic(s)** – e.g. `education.assignment`, `identity.session`, `gamification.badge`.\n    47→2. **Events are immutable** JSON messages, versioned via a `schemaVersion` field; schema registry enforced at the gateway.\n    48→3. **Append-only**: write operations emit events; read models are derived via consumer projections.\n    49→4. **Exactly-once** semantics: achieved by Kafka idempotent producers + transactional writes to outbox tables when necessary.\n    50→5. **Snapshot strategy**: Long-lived aggregates publish `*.snapshot` events every *n* revisions to shorten replay time.\n    51→6. **Backward compatibility**: Services must ignore unknown fields and can only add optional fields to existing event versions.\n    52→\n    53→---\n    54→\n    55→## 3. Consequences\n    56→\n    57→Positive:\n    58→\n    59→* **Auditability** – every state change is traceable.  \n    60→* **Resilience** – consumers can restart and replay without data loss.  \n    61→* **Scalable read models** – each service builds its own query-optimized projection.  \n    62→\n    63→Negative / Trade-offs:\n    64→\n    65→* **Write complexity** – commands must succeed or be compensated by new events.  \n    66→* **Learning curve** – developers must understand eventual consistency.  \n    67→* **Storage cost** – Kafka cluster size grows with retained history.  \n    68→\n    69→---\n    70→\n    71→## 4. Implementation Sketch (JavaScript / Node.js)\n    72→\n    73→Below are cut-down excerpts from production code to guide contributors.  \n    74→All examples assume Node.js ≥ 18, TypeScript, and the `kafkajs` client.\n    75→\n    76→### 4.1 Common Infrastructure\n    77→\n    78→```ts\n    79→// src/infrastructure/kafka/kafkaClient.ts\n    80→import { Kafka, logLevel } from 'kafkajs';\n 110→  type           : z.literal('AssignmentSubmitted'),\n   111→  occurredAt     : z.string().datetime({ offset: true }),\n   112→  payload        : z.object({\n   113→    courseId  : z.string().uuid(),\n   114→    studentId : z.string().uuid(),\n   115→    fileUrl   : z.string().url(),\n   116→  }),\n   117→});\n   118→\n   119→export type AssignmentSubmittedEvent = z.infer<typeof AssignmentSubmittedEventSchema>;\n   120→```\n   121→\n   122→### 4.3 Producer (Command Handler -> Event)\n   123→\n   124→```ts\n   125→// src/application/commands/submitAssignment.ts\n   126→import : randomUUID(),\n   141→    type          : 'AssignmentSubmitted',\n   142→    occurredAt    : new Date().toISOString(),\n   143→    payload       : cmd,\n   144→  };\n   145→\n   146→  const producer     messages: [\n   153→        {\n   154→          key  : event.aggregateId,\n   155→          value: JSON.stringify(event),\n   156→          headers: {\n   157→            'schema-version': '1',\n   158→            'event-type'    : event.type,\n   159→          },\n   160→        },\n   161→      ],\n   162→    });\n   163→  } finally {\n   164→    await producer.disconnect();\n   165→  }\n   166→}\n   167→```\n   168→\n   169→### 4.4 Consumer Projection (Gamification Points)\n   170→\n   171→```ts\n   172→// src/projections/gamification/pointsProjector.ts\n   173→import { kafka, topicName Context ‑ Why change?\n\nPulseLearn Campus Hub (PCH) is experiencing rapid feature growth:\n\n* Real-time dashboards for quiz progress, peer-review feedback, and badge awards  \n* A new gamification engine that recalculates points on every learning event  \n* Audit-ready reporting for compliance and accreditation bodies  \n\nThe existing CRUD-centric model has hit several limits:\n\n| Pain-Point                          | Symptom                                                                             |\n|------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| Data-coupling                       | Multiple bounded contexts write to the same tables, causing noisy merge conflicts. |\n| Missed notifications                | Lost WebSocket messages because they were dispatched directly from HTTP handlers.   |\n| Operational complexity              | Replaying history (e.g., to debug ranking errors) requires fragile SQL scripts.     |\n| Lack of temporal data               | Hard to answer “What was Alice’s score at 14:01 yesterday?”                         |\n\nTo address these, we want:\n\n1. **Immutable event log** – the single source of truth  \n2. **Replayability** – rebuild read-models at any point in time  \n3. **Loose coupling** – services subscribe to what they need, when they need it  \n4. **Scalability** – horizontal partitioning of event streams  \n\nApache **Kafka** is already used in our data-analytics pipeline; adopting it as the system event backbone is a natural next step.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Decision\n\nWe will adopt **event sourcing** for all new domain workflows and gradually migrate existing ones.  \nKafka will act as the **persistent event store** (commit log) and **message bus**.  \nSpecifics:\n\n1. **Each bounded context owns its topic(s)** – e.g. `education.assignment`, `identity.session`, `gamification.badge`.\n2. **Events are immutable** JSON messages, versioned via a `schemaVersion` field; schema registry enforced at the gateway.\n3. **Append-only**: write operations emit events; read models are derived via consumer projections.\n4. **Exactly-once** semantics: achieved by Kafka idempotent producers + transactional writes to outbox tables when necessary.\n5. **Snapshot strategy**: Long-lived aggregates publish `*.snapshot` events every *n* revisions to shorten replay time.\n6. **Backward compatibility**: Services must ignore unknown fields and can only add optional fields to existing event versions.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Consequences\n\nPositive:\n\n* **Auditability** – every state change is traceable.  \n* **Resilience** – consumers can restart and replay without data loss.  \n* **Scalable read models** – each service builds its own query-optimized projection.  \n\nNegative / Trade-offs:\n\n* **Write complexity** – commands must succeed or be compensated by new events.  \n* **Learning curve** – developers must understand eventual consistency.  \n* **Storage cost** – Kafka cluster size grows with retained history.  \n\n---\n\n## 4. Implementation Sketch (JavaScript / Node.js)\n\nBelow are cut-down excerpts from production code to guide contributors.  \nAll examples assume Node.js ≥ 18, TypeScript, and the `kafkajs` client.\n\n### 4.1 Common Infrastructure\n\n```ts\n// src/infrastructure/kafka/kafkaClient.ts\nimport { Kafka, logLevel } from 'kafkajs';\nimport config from '../../config';\n\nexport const kafka = new Kafka({\n  clientId: 'pulselearn-campus-hub',\n  brokers : config.kafka.brokers,\n  ssl     : true,\n  sasl    : {\n    mechanism : 'scram-sha-256',\n    username  : config.kafka.username,\n    password  : config.kafka.password,\n  },\n  logLevel: logLevel.WARN,\n});\n\nexport function topicName(boundedContext: string, suffix = ''): string {\n  return `${config.env}.${boundedContext}${suffix && `.${suffix}`}`;\n}\n```\n\n### 4.2 Event Contracts\n\n```ts\n// src/domain/events/AssignmentSubmittedEvent.ts\nimport { z } from 'zod';\n\nexport const AssignmentSubmittedEventSchema = z.object({\n  schemaVersion  : z.literal(1),\n  eventId        : z.string().uuid(),\n  aggregateId    : z.string().uuid(),   // Assignment ID\n  type           : z.literal('AssignmentSubmitted'),\n  occurredAt     : z.string().datetime({ offset: true }),\n  payload        : z.object({\n    courseId  : z.string().uuid(),\n    studentId : z.string().uuid(),\n    fileUrl   : z.string().url(),\n  }),\n});\n\nexport type AssignmentSubmittedEvent = z.infer<typeof AssignmentSubmittedEventSchema>;\n```\n\n### 4.3 Producer (Command Handler -> Event)\n\n```ts\n// src/application/commands/submitAssignment.ts\nimport { AssignmentSubmittedEvent } from '../../domain/events/AssignmentSubmittedEvent';\nimport { kafka, topicName } from '../../infrastructure/kafka/kafkaClient';\nimport { randomUUID } from 'crypto';\n\nexport async function submitAssignment(cmd: {\n  courseId: string;\n  studentId: string;\n  fileUrl: string;\n}): Promise<void> {\n  // Business validation omitted for brevity\n\n  const event: AssignmentSubmittedEvent = {\n    schemaVersion : 1,\n    eventId       : randomUUID(),\n    aggregateId   : randomUUID(),\n    type          : 'AssignmentSubmitted',\n    occurredAt    : new Date().toISOString(),\n    payload       : cmd,\n  };\n\n  const producer = kafka.producer({ idempotent: true });\n  await producer.connect();\n\n  try {\n    await producer.send({\n      topic: topicName('education.assignment'),\n      messages: [\n        {\n          key  : event.aggregateId,\n          value: JSON.stringify(event),\n          headers: {\n            'schema-version': '1',\n            'event-type'    : event.type,\n          },\n        },\n      ],\n    });\n  } finally {\n    await producer.disconnect();\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### 4.4 Consumer Projection (Gamification Points)\n\n```ts\n// src/projections/gamification/pointsProjector.ts\nimport { kafka, topicName } from '../../infrastructure/kafka/kafkaClient';\nimport { AssignmentSubmittedEventSchema } from '../../domain/events/AssignmentSubmittedEvent';\nimport pointsRepo from '../../repositories/pointsRepository';\n\nexport async function runPointsProjector(): Promise<void> {\n  const consumer = kafka.consumer({\n    groupId: 'gamification-points-projector-v1',\n  });\n\n  await consumer.connect();\n  await consumer.subscribe({\n    topic: topicName('education.assignment'),\n    fromBeginning: false,\n  });\n\n  await consumer.run({\n    eachMessage: async ({ message, partition }) => {\n      try {\n        const raw = message.value?.toString() ?? '{}';\n        const parsed = AssignmentSubmittedEventSchema.parse(JSON.parse(raw));\n\n        await pointsRepo.increment(\n          parsed.payload.studentId,\n          /* points = */ 5,\n        );\n\n        console.info(\n          `[PointProjector] +5 for student=${parsed.payload.studentId} (partition=${partition})`,\n        );\n      } catch (err) {\n        // DLQ or retry logic could be implemented here\n        console.error('[PointProjector] Failed to process message', err);\n      }\n    },\n  });\n}\n```\n\n### 4.5 Snapshotter Utility\n\n```ts\n// src/utils/snapshotter.ts\nimport { kafka, topicName } from '../infrastructure/kafka/kafkaClient';\n\nexport async function publishSnapshot<T>({\n  boundedContext,\n  aggregateName,\n  aggregateId,\n  revision,\n  state,\n}: {\n  boundedContext: string;\n  aggregateName: string;\n  aggregateId: string;\n  revision: number;\n  state: T;\n}): Promise<void> {\n  const producer = kafka.producer();\n\n  await producer.connect();\n  try {\n    await producer.send({\n      topic: topicName(`${boundedContext}.${aggregateName}.snapshot`),\n      messages: [\n        {\n          key  : aggregateId,\n          value: JSON.stringify({\n            schemaVersion: 1,\n            type         : `${aggregateName}.snapshot`,\n            revision,\n            occurredAt   : new Date().toISOString(),\n            state,\n          }),\n        },\n      ],\n    });\n  } finally {\n    await producer.disconnect();\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Migration Path\n\n1. **Brown-field integration**: legacy CRUD modules will emit events in a “change-data-capture” style, then slowly migrate command handling logic.  \n2. **Feature toggles**: Read models behind `/v2/` API routes will use event projections; `/v1/` remains on relational reads.  \n3. **Runbook & SRE**: New runbooks added for Kafka cluster monitoring (Lag, ISR, Disk usage).  \n4. **Developer enablement**: Internal workshop + “Event Sourcing 101” wiki page.  \n\n---\n\n## 6. Rejected Alternatives\n\n* **RabbitMQ + Outbox pattern** – flexible routing but lacks strong log-compaction and replay features.  \n* **Full CQRS without Event Sourcing** – improves reads but doesn’t solve auditability or temporal requirements.  \n\n---\n\n## 7. Open Issues / Next Steps\n\n1. Define retention policy per topic (regulatory requirement: 5-year minimum).  \n2. Agree on company-wide naming convention for events (`<Domain>.<Action><PastTense>`).  \n3. Evaluate Confluent Schema Registry vs. open-source alternatives.  \n4. Prototype snapshotting performance with 1M-event aggregate.  \n\n---\n\n> “If you treat data as immutable facts, your systems become a chronicle of truth, not just a reflection of the present.” –  Tech Lead, PCH\n```","numLines":283,"startLine":1,"totalLines":283}}}

Context  \n    13→\n    14→PulseLearn Campus Hub (PL-CH) must enable:\n    15→\n    16→1. Real-time, bidirectional interactions between thousands of concurrent users (students, teachers, admins).  \n    17→2. Rapid feature evolution by autonomous domain-centric teams.  \n    18→3. Fault-tolerance and graceful degradation of non-critical functionality (e.g., delayed “BadgeAwarded” events must **not** block quiz submissions).  \n    19→4. Polyglot technology choices while enforcing a consistent ops/observability contract.  \n    20→5. Compliance with FERPA, GDPR, and internal audit requirements such as end-to-end traceability and PII redaction policies.\n    21→\n    22→The alternatives considered were:\n    23→\n    24→| Candidate                     | Pros                                                                                                    | Cons                                                                                                   |\n    25→| ----------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n    26→| A) Single-service (monolith)  | Simple to deploy, transactional consistency, easier local dev                                           | Scalability bottlenecks, slower team velocity, long-lived branches, risky deployments                  |\n    27→| B) Self-contained services    | Autonomy per bounded context, independent UI + API bundles                                              | Risk of UI duplication, heavier front-end orchestration                                                |\n    28→| **C) Microservices + Event Bus** | Loose coupling via events, horizontal scalability, team autonomy, tech polyglot, easier domain modeling | Requires distributed tracing, eventual consistency semantics, operational complexity                   |\n    29→| D) Serverless (FaaS only)     | Zero infra management, cost per execution                                                               | Cold starts, vendor lock-in, limited long-running connections, complex local testing                   |\n    30→\n    31→After evaluating traffic patterns, compliance constraints, and organisation structure (5 cross-functional squads), option **C** was chosen.\n    32→\n    33→---\n    34→\n    35→## 2. Decision  \n    36→\n    37→We will build **PulseLearn Campus Hub** as an **event-driven microservices system**:\n    38→\n    39→1. **Domain-Oriented Microservices**  \n    40→   • Each service owns a bounded context & data store (e.g., `UserProfile`, `LearningPath`, `Achievement`, `Notification`).  \n    41→   • Services communicate asynchronously via a Kafka compatible bus (NATS JetStream in staging, Confluent Cloud in prod).  \n    42→   • REST/gRPC APIs remain for synchronous “query” use-cases and external integrations (admin panel, LTI).  \n    43→\n    44→2. **Event Taxonomy**  \n    45→   • All domain events adopt the CloudEvents 1.0 spec.  \n    46→   • Topic naming: `pl.<boundedContext>.<eventName>.<version>` (e.g., `pl.achievement.badge_awarded.v1`).  \n    47→   • Payloads are validated with versioned Avro schemas stored in Schema Registry.  \n    48→\n    49→3. **Shared Platform Libraries**  \n    50→   • We ship an OSS-friendly **`@pl/platform`** JavaScript package that wraps NATS/Kafka producers & consumers, tracing, and idempotency utilities (see snippet below).  \n    51→   • Analogous libraries will exist for Python, Go, and Java to keep polyglot parity.  \n    52→\n    53→4. **Observability & Resilience**  \n    54→   • OpenTelemetry for distributed tracing, correlated logs, metrics (`traceId` + `spanId` pass via W3C `traceparent`).  \n    55→   • Circuit breakers (opossum) guard cross-service HTTP calls.  \n    56→   • Retry, DLQ, and “parking lot” patterns for poison messages.  \n    57→\n    58→5. **Security**  \n    59→   • mTLS between services (Linkerd service mesh).  \n    60→   • SPIFFE IDs for zero-trust identity.  \n    61→   • PII encrypted at rest (PostgreSQL + envelope encryption).  \n    62→\n    63→---\n    64→\n    65→## 3. Consequences  \n    66→\n    67→Positive:  \n    68→✓ Independent deployability enables <15 min release cadence per squad.  \n    69→✓ Horizontal scaling by workload (e.g., `RecommendationService` auto-scales via KEDA based on Kafka lag).  \n    70→✓ Failure isolation—`SearchIndexer` crash has no direct impact on “QuizCompletion” UX.  \n    71→\n    72→Negative:  \n    73→✗ Added operational overhead: CI/CD pipelines, playbooks, Terraform modules per service.  \n    74→✗ Data consistency becomes eventual; we must adopt Saga patterns for workflows like “PurchaseCourse → ActivateAccess → EmitInvoice”.  \n    75→✗ On-call complexity: PagerDuty rotations per squad, plus a shared SRE roster for platform components (Kafka, Linkerd, Istio ingress).\n    76→\n    77→---\n    78→\n    79→## 4. Implementation Sketch  \n    80→\n    81→### Mermaid: High-level view\n    82→\n    83→```mermaid\n    84→flowchart LR\n    85→    subgraph Frontend\n    86→        ReactApp[Web SPA]\n    87→        MobileApp[React Native]\n    88→    end\n    89→    ReactApp -->|REST: /api/v1| APIGateway\n    90→    MobileApp -->|GraphQL| APIGateway\n    91→    APIGateway --> AuthSvc[(Auth Service)]\n    92→    APIGateway --> UserSvc[(User Profile)]\n    93→    APIGateway --> CourseSvc[(Course Catalog)]\n    94→    style APIGateway fill:#f5f5f5,stroke:#333,stroke-width:1px\n    95→    \n    96→    %% Event -->|pl.user.session_expired| NATS\n   103→    NATS --> SearchSvc[(Search Indexer)]\n   104→    NATS --> BadgeSvc[(Achievement)]\n   105→    NATS --> RecSvc[(Recommendation)]\n   106→```\n   107→\n   108→### Shared JS Event Library (`@pl/platform`)\n   109→\n   110→```javascript\n   111→// @pl/platform/lib/event-bus.js\n   112→import { connect, StringCodec } from 'nats';\n   113→import { v4 as 119→export class EventBus {\n   120→  static instance;\n   121→\n   122→  /**\n   123→   * Initialise a NATS connection (lazy).\n   124→   * @param {object} config - {servers: string, jwt?: string, console.info('[EventBus] Connected to NATS');\n   144→    } catch (err) {\n   145→      console.error('[EventBus] Connection error', err);\n   146→      throw err;\n   147→    }\n   148→  }\n   149→\n   150→  /**\n   151→   * Publish a CloudEvent to the given topic.\n   152→   * @param {string} topic - e.g. \"pl.achievement.badge_awarded.v1\"\n   153→   * @param {object} data - event payload\n   154→   * @param {object} [options] - headers, trace context, etc.\n   155→   */\n   156→  async publish(topic, data, options = {}) {\n   157→    if (!this.nc) throw new Error('EventBus not initialised');\n   158→    const span = otel.trace.getTracer('pl-platform').startSpan(`publish:${topic}`);\n   159→    try {\n   160→      const event = {\n   161→        id: uuid(),\n   162→ this.sc.encode(JSON.stringify(event)), { headers });\n   172→      span.setStatus({ code: otel.SpanStatusCode.OK });\n   173→    } catch (err) {\n   174→      span.setStatus({ code: otel.SpanStatusCode.ERROR, message: err.message });\n   175→      throw err;\n   176→    } finally {\n   177→      span.end();\n   178→    }\n   179→  }\n   180→\n   181→  /**\n   182→   * Subscribe to a topic with durable consumer semantics.\n   183→   * @param {string} topic\n   184→   * @param {string} durableName\n   185→   * @param {(evt: object) => Promise<void>} handler\n   186→   */\n   187→  async subscribe(topic, durableName, handler) {\n   188→    const subOpts = { durable: durableName, manualAck: true, ackWait: 60_000 };\n   189→    const sub = await this.js.subscribe(topic, subOpts);\n   190→    console.info(`[EventBus] Subscribed to ${topic}`);\n   191→\n   192→    (async () => {\n   193→      for await (const m of sub) {\n   194→        const span = otel.trace.getTracer('pl-platform').startSpan(`consume:${topic}`);\n   195→        try {\n   196→          const evt = JSON.parse(this.sc.decode(m.data));\n   197→          await handler(evt);\n   198→          m.ack();\n   199→          span.setStatus({ code: otel.SpanStatusCode.OK });\n   200→        } catch (err) {\n   201→          console.error('[EventBus] Handler error', err);\n   202→ span.end();\n   206→        }\n   207→      }\n   208→    })().catch((err) => console.error('[EventBus] Async iteration error', err));\n   209→  }\n   210→\n   211→  _buildHeaders(span, extra = {}) {\n   212→    const headers = this.nc.headers();\n   213→    const ctx = otel.trace.setSpan(otel.context.active(), span);\n   214→    otel.propagation.inject(ctx, headers, {\n   215→      set: (carrier, key, value) => carrier.set(key, value),\n   216→    });\n   217→    Object.entries(extra).forEach(([k, v]) => headers.set(k, v));\n   218→    return headers;\n   219→  }\n   220→}\n   221→```\n   222→\n   223→### Saga Orchestration Example (Enroll Premium Course)\n   224→\n   225→```javascript\n   226→// services/payment/src/enrollCourseSaga.js\n   227→import { EventBus } from '@pl/platform';\n   228→import { db } from '../db';\n   229→import { generateInvoice } from '../billing';\n   230→import { logger } from '../logger';\n   231→\n   232→export async function registerEventHandlers() {\n   233→  const bus = await EventBus.init({ servers: process.env.NATS_URL });\n   234→\n   235→  // listen to PaymentCaptured\n   236→  await bus.subscribe('pl.payment.captured.v1', 'enroll-premium-course', async (evt) => {\n   237→    const { userId, courseId, paymentId } = evt.data;\n   238→    logger.info('Payment captured', { userId, courseId, paymentId });\n   239→\n   240→    // Step 1: Activate access in course service (synchronous call)\n   241→    try {\n   242→      await activateCourseAccess(userId, courseId);\n   243→    } catch (err) {\n   244→      logger.error('Course activation failed', err);\n   245→      // emit compensating event\n   246→      await bus.publish('pl.payment.refund_requested.v1', { paymentId, reason: 'COURSE_ACTIVATION_FAILED' });\n   247→      return;\n   248→    }\n   249→\n   250→    // Step 2: Emit EnrollmentGranted event\n   251→    await bus.publish('pl.enrollment.granted.v1', { userId, courseId });\n   252→\n   253→    // Step 3: Issue invoice (local DB + external PDF generator)\n   254→    try {\n   255→      const invoiceId = await generateInvoice({ userId, paymentId });\n   256→      await db.invoice.markSent(invoiceId);\n   257→    } catch (err) {\n   258→      logger.error('Invoice generation failed', err);\n   259→      // Non-blocking; instructor & finance will be alerted via DLQ consumer\n   260→    }\n   261→  });\n   262→}\n   263→```\n   264→\n   265→---\n   266→\n   267→## 5. Alternatives Explored (But NOT Chosen)\n   268→\n   269→* **Monolith + Event Module** – insufficient isolation; high coordination cost for multi-squad development.  \n   270→* **Pure Serverless** – latency and unpredictable cost for high-frequency message streams (quizzes, edits).  \n   271→* **gRPC-only Service Mesh** – synchronous coupling failed chaos testing when `AnalyticsService` induced 40 % error rate.\n   272→\n   273→---\n   274→\n   275→## 6. Validation & Next Steps\n   276→\n   277→1. Platform Team to spin up NATS + JetStream in staging (k8s operator).  \n   278→2. Each squad migrates at least one bounded context to the new pattern by Sprint 14.  \n   279→3. Evaluate Confluent vs Redpanda for prod by Q3.  \n   280→4. Conduct load test: 50k concurrent websocket sessions, publish 2k events/s, < 200 ms P99 end-to-end latency.  \n   281→\n   282→---\n   283→\n   284→## 7. References\n   285→\n   286→* “Building Event-Driven Microservices” – O’Reilly, 2021  \n   287→* CloudEvents 1.0 Spec – https://cloudevents.io/  \n   288→* JetStream Acknowledgements – https://docs.nats.io/jetstream/consumers/ack_policy  \n   289→* Saga Pattern – https://microservices.io/patterns/data/saga.html  \n   290→\n   291→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. Context  \n\nPulseLearn Campus Hub (PL-CH) must enable:\n\n1. Real-time, bidirectional interactions between thousands of concurrent users (students, teachers, admins).  \n2. Rapid feature evolution by autonomous domain-centric teams.  \n3. Fault-tolerance and graceful degradation of non-critical functionality (e.g., delayed “BadgeAwarded” events must **not** block quiz submissions).  \n4. Polyglot technology choices while enforcing a consistent ops/observability contract.  \n5. Compliance with FERPA, GDPR, and internal audit requirements such as end-to-end traceability and PII redaction policies.\n\nThe alternatives considered were:\n\n| Candidate                     | Pros                                                                                                    | Cons                                                                                                   |\n| ----------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| A) Single-service (monolith)  | Simple to deploy, transactional consistency, easier local dev                                           | Scalability bottlenecks, slower team velocity, long-lived branches, risky deployments                  |\n| B) Self-contained services    | Autonomy per bounded context, independent UI + API bundles                                              | Risk of UI duplication, heavier front-end orchestration                                                |\n| **C) Microservices + Event Bus** | Loose coupling via events, horizontal scalability, team autonomy, tech polyglot, easier domain modeling | Requires distributed tracing, eventual consistency semantics, operational complexity                   |\n| D) Serverless (FaaS only)     | Zero infra management, cost per execution                                                               | Cold starts, vendor lock-in, limited long-running connections, complex local testing                   |\n\nAfter evaluating traffic patterns, compliance constraints, and organisation structure (5 cross-functional squads), option **C** was chosen.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Decision  \n\nWe will build **PulseLearn Campus Hub** as an **event-driven microservices system**:\n\n1. **Domain-Oriented Microservices**  \n   • Each service owns a bounded context & data store (e.g., `UserProfile`, `LearningPath`, `Achievement`, `Notification`).  \n   • Services communicate asynchronously via a Kafka compatible bus (NATS JetStream in staging, Confluent Cloud in prod).  \n   • REST/gRPC APIs remain for synchronous “query” use-cases and external integrations (admin panel, LTI).  \n\n2. **Event Taxonomy**  \n   • All domain events adopt the CloudEvents 1.0 spec.  \n   • Topic naming: `pl.<boundedContext>.<eventName>.<version>` (e.g., `pl.achievement.badge_awarded.v1`).  \n   • Payloads are validated with versioned Avro schemas stored in Schema Registry.  \n\n3. **Shared Platform Libraries**  \n   • We ship an OSS-friendly **`@pl/platform`** JavaScript package that wraps NATS/Kafka producers & consumers, tracing, and idempotency utilities (see snippet below).  \n   • Analogous libraries will exist for Python, Go, and Java to keep polyglot parity.  \n\n4. **Observability & Resilience**  \n   • OpenTelemetry for distributed tracing, correlated logs, metrics (`traceId` + `spanId` pass via W3C `traceparent`).  \n   • Circuit breakers (opossum) guard cross-service HTTP calls.  \n   • Retry, DLQ, and “parking lot” patterns for poison messages.  \n\n5. **Security**  \n   • mTLS between services (Linkerd service mesh).  \n   • SPIFFE IDs for zero-trust identity.  \n   • PII encrypted at rest (PostgreSQL + envelope encryption).  \n\n---\n\n## 3. Consequences  \n\nPositive:  \n✓ Independent deployability enables <15 min release cadence per squad.  \n✓ Horizontal scaling by workload (e.g., `RecommendationService` auto-scales via KEDA based on Kafka lag).  \n✓ Failure isolation—`SearchIndexer` crash has no direct impact on “QuizCompletion” UX.  \n\nNegative:  \n✗ Added operational overhead: CI/CD pipelines, playbooks, Terraform modules per service.  \n✗ Data consistency becomes eventual; we must adopt Saga patterns for workflows like “PurchaseCourse → ActivateAccess → EmitInvoice”.  \n✗ On-call complexity: PagerDuty rotations per squad, plus a shared SRE roster for platform components (Kafka, Linkerd, Istio ingress).\n\n---\n\n## 4. Implementation Sketch  \n\n### Mermaid: High-level view\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n    subgraph Frontend\n        ReactApp[Web SPA]\n        MobileApp[React Native]\n    end\n    ReactApp -->|REST: /api/v1| APIGateway\n    MobileApp -->|GraphQL| APIGateway\n    APIGateway --> AuthSvc[(Auth Service)]\n    APIGateway --> UserSvc[(User Profile)]\n    APIGateway --> CourseSvc[(Course Catalog)]\n    style APIGateway fill:#f5f5f5,stroke:#333,stroke-width:1px\n    \n    %% Event Bus\n    subgraph EventBus[\"NATS / Kafka\"]\n        NATS[(JetStream Topic)]\n    end\n    \n    CourseSvc -->|pl.course.uploaded| NATS\n    UserSvc -->|pl.user.session_expired| NATS\n    NATS --> SearchSvc[(Search Indexer)]\n    NATS --> BadgeSvc[(Achievement)]\n    NATS --> RecSvc[(Recommendation)]\n```\n\n### Shared JS Event Library (`@pl/platform`)\n\n```javascript\n// @pl/platform/lib/event-bus.js\nimport { connect, StringCodec } from 'nats';\nimport { v4 as uuid } from 'uuid';\nimport * as otel from '@opentelemetry/api';\n\n/**\n * EventBus singleton to publish/subscribe CloudEvents-compliant messages.\n */\nexport class EventBus {\n  static instance;\n\n  /**\n   * Initialise a NATS connection (lazy).\n   * @param {object} config - {servers: string, jwt?: string, nkey?: string}\n   */\n  static async init(config) {\n    if (!EventBus.instance) {\n      EventBus.instance = new EventBus(config);\n      await EventBus.instance._connect();\n    }\n    return EventBus.instance;\n  }\n\n  constructor(config) {\n    this.config = config;\n    this.sc = StringCodec();\n  }\n\n  async _connect() {\n    try {\n      this.nc = await connect(this.config);\n      this.js = this.nc.jetstream();\n      console.info('[EventBus] Connected to NATS');\n    } catch (err) {\n      console.error('[EventBus] Connection error', err);\n      throw err;\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Publish a CloudEvent to the given topic.\n   * @param {string} topic - e.g. \"pl.achievement.badge_awarded.v1\"\n   * @param {object} data - event payload\n   * @param {object} [options] - headers, trace context, etc.\n   */\n  async publish(topic, data, options = {}) {\n    if (!this.nc) throw new Error('EventBus not initialised');\n    const span = otel.trace.getTracer('pl-platform').startSpan(`publish:${topic}`);\n    try {\n      const event = {\n        id: uuid(),\n        source: 'pulselearn-campus-hub',\n        specversion: '1.0',\n        type: topic,\n        time: new Date().toISOString(),\n        datacontenttype: 'application/json',\n        data,\n      };\n      // propagate trace context\n      const headers = this._buildHeaders(span, options.headers);\n      await this.js.publish(topic, this.sc.encode(JSON.stringify(event)), { headers });\n      span.setStatus({ code: otel.SpanStatusCode.OK });\n    } catch (err) {\n      span.setStatus({ code: otel.SpanStatusCode.ERROR, message: err.message });\n      throw err;\n    } finally {\n      span.end();\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Subscribe to a topic with durable consumer semantics.\n   * @param {string} topic\n   * @param {string} durableName\n   * @param {(evt: object) => Promise<void>} handler\n   */\n  async subscribe(topic, durableName, handler) {\n    const subOpts = { durable: durableName, manualAck: true, ackWait: 60_000 };\n    const sub = await this.js.subscribe(topic, subOpts);\n    console.info(`[EventBus] Subscribed to ${topic}`);\n\n    (async () => {\n      for await (const m of sub) {\n        const span = otel.trace.getTracer('pl-platform').startSpan(`consume:${topic}`);\n        try {\n          const evt = JSON.parse(this.sc.decode(m.data));\n          await handler(evt);\n          m.ack();\n          span.setStatus({ code: otel.SpanStatusCode.OK });\n        } catch (err) {\n          console.error('[EventBus] Handler error', err);\n          // let JetStream redeliver, or add to DLQ via a header\n          span.setStatus({ code: otel.SpanStatusCode.ERROR, message: err.message });\n        } finally {\n          span.end();\n        }\n      }\n    })().catch((err) => console.error('[EventBus] Async iteration error', err));\n  }\n\n  _buildHeaders(span, extra = {}) {\n    const headers = this.nc.headers();\n    const ctx = otel.trace.setSpan(otel.context.active(), span);\n    otel.propagation.inject(ctx, headers, {\n      set: (carrier, key, value) => carrier.set(key, value),\n    });\n    Object.entries(extra).forEach(([k, v]) => headers.set(k, v));\n    return headers;\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Saga Orchestration Example (Enroll Premium Course)\n\n```javascript\n// services/payment/src/enrollCourseSaga.js\nimport { EventBus } from '@pl/platform';\nimport { db } from '../db';\nimport { generateInvoice } from '../billing';\nimport { logger } from '../logger';\n\nexport async function registerEventHandlers() {\n  const bus = await EventBus.init({ servers: process.env.NATS_URL });\n\n  // listen to PaymentCaptured\n  await bus.subscribe('pl.payment.captured.v1', 'enroll-premium-course', async (evt) => {\n    const { userId, courseId, paymentId } = evt.data;\n    logger.info('Payment captured', { userId, courseId, paymentId });\n\n    // Step 1: Activate access in course service (synchronous call)\n    try {\n      await activateCourseAccess(userId, courseId);\n    } catch (err) {\n      logger.error('Course activation failed', err);\n      // emit compensating event\n      await bus.publish('pl.payment.refund_requested.v1', { paymentId, reason: 'COURSE_ACTIVATION_FAILED' });\n      return;\n    }\n\n    // Step 2: Emit EnrollmentGranted event\n    await bus.publish('pl.enrollment.granted.v1', { userId, courseId });\n\n    // Step 3: Issue invoice (local DB + external PDF generator)\n    try {\n      const invoiceId = await generateInvoice({ userId, paymentId });\n      await db.invoice.markSent(invoiceId);\n    } catch (err) {\n      logger.error('Invoice generation failed', err);\n      // Non-blocking; instructor & finance will be alerted via DLQ consumer\n    }\n  });\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Alternatives Explored (But NOT Chosen)\n\n* **Monolith + Event Module** – insufficient isolation; high coordination cost for multi-squad development.  \n* **Pure Serverless** – latency and unpredictable cost for high-frequency message streams (quizzes, edits).  \n* **gRPC-only Service Mesh** – synchronous coupling failed chaos testing when `AnalyticsService` induced 40 % error rate.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Validation & Next Steps\n\n1. Platform Team to spin up NATS + JetStream in staging (k8s operator).  \n2. Each squad migrates at least one bounded context to the new pattern by Sprint 14.  \n3. Evaluate Confluent vs Redpanda for prod by Q3.  \n4. Conduct load test: 50k concurrent websocket sessions, publish 2k events/s, < 200 ms P99 end-to-end latency.  \n\n---\n\n## 7. References\n\n* “Building Event-Driven Microservices” – O’Reilly, 2021  \n* CloudEvents 1.0 Spec – https://cloudevents.io/  \n* JetStream Acknowledgements – https://docs.nats.io/jetstream/consumers/ack_policy  \n* Saga Pattern – https://microservices.io/patterns/data/saga.html  \n\n```","numLines":291,"startLine":1,"totalLines":291}}}

* PulseLearn Campus Hub – Dynamic Nginx Configuration Builder\n * -----------------------------------------------------------\n * This script is responsible for generating a robust, battle-tested\n * nginx.conf file that fronts the API-Gateway of the PulseLearn platform.\n *\n * Why JavaScript for an “nginx.conf”?\n * ----------------------------------\n * • Configuration needs to adapt to dynamic container / k8s deployments.\n * • Runtime values (hostnames, ports, ssl paths) come from ENV vars.\n * • We want validation, helpful error messages and unit-testability.\n *\n * Usage\n * -----\n *  $ node nginx.conf [output-path]\n *\n *  – If executed via `node`, the generated configuration is written to\n *    the provided output path (default: /etc/nginx/conf.d/pulselearn.conf)\n *  – When imported (`require('./nginx.conf')`) the `buildNginxConfig`\n *    method returns the config as a string.\n */\n\n'use strict';\n\nconst fs   = require('fs');\nconst path = require('path');\nconst os   = require('os');\n\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n// Environment Schema\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n\nconst ENV_SCHEMA = {\n  /** Comma-separated hosts for each micro-service.\n   *  Example: AUTH_SERVICE_HOSTS=\"auth1:4000,auth2:4000\" */\n  AUTH_SERVICE_HOSTS        : { required: true  },\n  USER_SERVICE_HOSTS        : { required: true  },\n  CONTENT_SERVICE_HOSTS     : { required: true  },\n  NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_HOSTS: { required: true  },\n\n  /** SSL certificate / key */\n  SSL_CERT_PATH             : { required: false, default: '/etc/ssl/certs/pulselearn.crt' },\n  SSL_KEY_PATH              : { required: false, default: '/etc/ssl/private/pulselearn.key' },\n\n  /** Optional toggles */\n  FORCE_SSL                 : { required: false, default: 'true' },\n  CLIENT_MAX_BODY_SIZE_MB   : { required: false, default: '20' },\n  RATE_LIMIT_REQ_PER_MIN    : { required: false, default: '600' },\n\n  /** Logging */\n  ACCESS_LOG_PATH           : { required: false, default: '/var/log/nginx/access.log' },\n  ERROR_LOG_PATH            : { required: false, default: '/var/log/nginx/error.log' }\n};\n\n/**\n * Load and validate environment variables against the schema.\n * Throws if a required variable is missing.\n */\nfunction loadEnv () {\n  const conf = {};\n\n  Object.entries(ENV_SCHEMA).forEach(([key, meta]) => {\n    const value = process.env[key] ?? meta.default;\n    if (meta.required && (value === undefined || value === null)) {\n      throw new Error(`Missing required environment variable: ${key}`);\n    }\n    conf[key] = value;\n  });\n\n  return conf;\n}\n\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n// Helper Functions\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n\n/**\n * Parse upstream hosts into nginx `server` directives.\n *\n * @param {string} hostsString - Comma-separated host:port pairs.\n * @returns {string}           - Multi-line string ready to embed.\n */\nfunction toUpstreamServers (hostsString) {\n  return hostsString\n    .split(',')\n    .map(host => host.trim())\n    .filter(Boolean)\n    .map(host => `        server ${host};`)\n    .join('\\n');\n}\n\n/**\n * Render a single upstream block.\n *\n * @param {string} name       - Upstream name (e.g. \"auth_service\").\n * @param {string} hosts      - Comma-separated list of host:port targets.\n * @param {object} [opts={}]  - Options like keepalive settings.\n */\nfunction renderUpstream (name, hosts, opts = {}) {\n  const keepalive = opts.keepalive ?? 64;\n  return `\n    upstream ${name} {\n${toUpstreamServers(hosts)}\n        keepalive ${keepalive};\n    }`;\n}\n\n/**\n * Render a location block that proxies to a given upstream service.\n *\n * @param {string} pathPrefix   - URI prefix (e.g. \"/auth/\").\n * @param {string} upstreamName - Name defined in an `upstream` block.\n */\nfunction renderLocation (pathPrefix, upstreamName) {\n  return `\n            location ${pathPrefix} {\n                proxy_pass          http://${upstreamName};\n                proxy_http_version  1.1;\n                proxy_set_header    Connection \"\";\n                proxy_set_header    Host              $host;\n                proxy_set_header    X-Real-IP         $remote_addr;\n                proxy_set_header    X-Forwarded-For   $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;\n                proxy_set_header    X-Forwarded-Proto $scheme;\n                proxy_set_header    X-Request-ID      $request_id;\n                proxy_cache_bypass  $cookie_session;\n            }`;\n}\n\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n// Main Config Builder\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n\n/**\n * Build the complete nginx.conf string.\n *\n * @returns {string} The final configuration.\n */\nfunction buildNginxConfig () {\n  const env = loadEnv();\n\n  const RATE_LIMIT_ZONE = `\n    limit_req_zone $binary_remote_addr zone=req_limit:${parseInt(env.RATE_LIMIT_REQ_PER_MIN, 10) * 32}k rate=${env.RATE_LIMIT_REQ_PER_MIN}r/m;`;\n\n  const UPSTREAMS = [\n    ['auth_service',          env.AUTH_SERVICE_HOSTS],\n    ['user_service',          env.USER_SERVICE_HOSTS],\n    ['content_service',       env.CONTENT_SERVICE_HOSTS],\n    ['notification_service',  env.NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_HOSTS]\n  ].map(([name, hosts]) => renderUpstream(name, hosts)).join('\\n');\n\n  const 'content_service')       +\n      renderLocation('/notifications/','notification_service');\n\n  const SSL_CONFIG = (env.FORCE_SSL === 'true')\n    ? `\n        listen 443 ssl http2;\n        ssl_certificate     ${env.SSL_CERT_PATH};\n        ssl_certificate_key ${env.SSL_KEY_PATH};\n        ssl_protocols       TLSv1.2 TLSv1.3;\n        ssl_ciphers         HIGH:!aNULL:!MD5;\n        ssl_prefer_server_ciphers on;\n        ssl_session_cache   shared:SSL:10m;\n        add_header Strict-Transport-Security \"max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains\" always;`\n    : 'listen 80;';\n\n  const REDIRECT_HTTP_TO_HTTPS = (env.FORCE_SSL === 'true')\n    ? `\n    server {\n        listen      80;\n        server_name _;\n        return 301 https://$host$request_uri;\n    }`\n    : '';\n\n  return `\n###############################################################################\n#  PulseLearn Campus Hub – Auto-Generated Nginx Configuration\n#  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#  This file was generated by services/api-gateway/nginx.conf (NodeJS script).\n#  DO NOT EDIT MANUALLY.
