It handles\n * resilience (retry, DLQ), observability (metrics, structured logs) and\n * graceful shutdown.\n *\n * File: services/payment-service/src/events/consumer.js\n * Author: PulseLearn Engineering\n */\n\n'use strict';\n\nconst { Kafka, logLevel } = require('kafkajs');\nconst { Counter, Histogram, Registry } = require('prom-client');\nconst pRetry = require('p-retry');\nconst createLogger = require('../utils/logger');\nconst config = require('../config');\nconst paymentService = require('../services/paymentProcessorService');\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Constants & Configuration\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nconst logger = createLogger({ service: 'payment-event-consumer' });\n\nconst {\n  kafka: { clientId, brokers, ssl, sasl, groupId },\n  paymentTopics,\n  instrumentation: { metricsPort },\n} = config;\n\nconst MAX_PROCESSING_RETRIES = 5;\nconst DLQ_TOPIC = paymentTopics.deadLetter;\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Metrics\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nconst registry = new Registry();\n\nconst messageReceivedCounter = new Counter({\n  name: 'payment_consumer_messages_total',\n  help: 'Total number of messages received',\n  registers: [registry],\n});\n\nconst messageProcessedCounter = new Counter({\n  name: 'payment_consumer_messages_processed_total',\n  help: 'Total number of messages successfully processed',\n  registers: [registry],\n});\n\nconst messageFailedCounter = new Counter({\n  name: 'payment_consumer_messages_failed_total',\n  help: 'Total number of messages that failed permanently',\n  registers: [registry],\n});\n\nconst processingLatencyHistogram = new Histogram({\n  name: 'payment_consumer_processing_latency_seconds',\n  help: 'Message processing latency in seconds',\n  buckets: [0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5],\n  registers: [registry],\n});\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Kafka Consumer Wrapper\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nclass PaymentEventConsumer {\n  constructor() {\n    this.kafka = new Kafka({\n      clientId,\n      brokers,\n      ssl,\n      sasl,\n      logLevel: logLevel.INFO,\n      logCreator: () => ({ namespace, level, label, log }) => {\n        logger.log({\n          level: level === logLevel.ERROR ? 'error' : 'info',\n          message: `[${namespace}] ${label} ${log.message}`,\n          extra: log,\n        });\n      },\n    });\n\n    this.consumer = this.kafka.consumer({ groupId });\n    this.producer = this.kafka.producer();\n    this.running = false;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Initialize consumer subscriptions\n   */\n  async init() {\n    await this.consumer.connect();\n    await this.producer.connect();\n\n    await Promise.all(\n      paymentTopics.inbound.map((topic) => this.consumer.subscribe({ topic, fromBeginning: false })),\n    );\n\n    logger.info(`Subscribed to payment topics: ${paymentTopics.inbound.join(', ')}`);\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Run the consumer loop\n   */\n  async run() {\n    this.running = true;\n\n    await this.consumer.run({\n      eachMessage: async ({ topic, partition, message }) => {\n        messageReceivedCounter.inc();\n        const start = process.hrtime.bigint();\n\n        try {\n          const parsed = JSON.parse(message.value.toString());\n          logger.debug('Received event', { topic, ...parsed });\n\n          await this._processEvent(parsed);\n          messageProcessedCounter.inc();\n        } catch (err) {\n          await this._handleProcessingError(topic, message, err);\n        } finally {\n          const diff = (Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - start) / 1e9);\n          processingLatencyHistogram.observe(diff);\n        }\n      },\n    });\n\n    logger.info('PaymentEventConsumer is running');\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Stop consumer gracefully\n   */\n  async shutdown() {\n    if (!this.running) return;\n    this.running = false;\n    logger.info('Shutting down PaymentEventConsumer ...');\n    await Promise.all([this.consumer.disconnect(), this.producer.disconnect()]);\n    logger.info('PaymentEventConsumer stopped');\n  }\n\n  // -----------------------------------------------------------------------\n  // Private utilities\n  // -----------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n  /**\n   * Route event to appropriate handler\n   * @param {Object} event\n   */\n  async _processEvent(event) {\n    const { type, data, metadata } = event;\n\n    if (!type) {\n      throw new Error('Event missing \"type\" property');\n    }\n\n    const handler = this._getHandlerForType(type);\n\n    if (!handler) {\n      logger.warn(`No handler implemented for event type \"${type}\" – ignored`);\n      return;\n    }\n\n    // Wrap business logic in retry policy\n    await pRetry(() => handler(data, metadata), {\n      retries: MAX_PROCESSING_RETRIES,\n      onFailedAttempt: (err) => {\n        logger.warn(\n          `Attempt ${err.attemptNumber} failed to process ${type}. ${err.retriesLeft} retries left.`,\n          { err },\n        );\n      },\n    });\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Resolve event type to service handler\n   */\n  _getHandlerForType(type) {\n    const map = {\n      PaymentInitiated: paymentService.handlePaymentInitiated,\n      PaymentSucceeded: paymentService.handlePaymentSucceeded,\n      PaymentFailed:    paymentService.handlePaymentFailed,\n      RefundRequested:  paymentService.handleRefundRequested,\n      CoursePurchased:  paymentService.handleCoursePurchased,\n    };\n    return map[type];\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Persist message to Dead-Letter-Queue and track metrics\n   */\n  async _handleProcessingError(topic, message, err) {\n    logger.error('Permanent failure processing message', { err });\n\n    messageFailedCounter.inc();\n\n    // Forward to DLQ (fire-and-forget)\n    try {\n      await this.producer.send({\n        topic: DLQ_TOPIC,\n        messages: [\n          {\n            key: message.key,\n            value: message.value,\n            headers: {\n              'x-original-topic': topic,\n              'x-error': Buffer.from(err.message).toString('base64'),\n            },\n          },\n        ],\n      });\n      logger.info('Message sent to DLQ', { DLQ_TOPIC });\n    } catch (dlqErr) {\n      logger.error('Failed to write to DLQ – message lost', { dlqErr });\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Bootstrap\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nconst consumer = new PaymentEventConsumer();\n\n(async () => {\n  try {\n    await consumer.init();\n    await consumer.run();\n  } catch (err) {\n    logger.error('Fatal error starting PaymentEventConsumer', { err });\n    process.exit(1);\n  }\n})();\n\nprocess\n  .once('SIGINT', async () => {\n    await consumer.shutdown();\n    process.exit(0);\n  })\n  .once('SIGTERM', async () => {\n    await We therefore evaluated several strategies:\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n| ------ | ---- | ---- |\n| 1. Single SQL RDBMS (PostgreSQL) | ACID, mature tooling | Hot partitions, expensive scaling, poor search |\n| 2. Single document DB (MongoDB) | Flexible schema, good read scaling | Event ordering issues, multi-doc transactions slower |\n| 3. Polyglot persistence *(selected)* | Fit-for-purpose stores, cost-effective | Operational complexity, cross-store transactions |\n\n## Decision  \nWe adopt **polyglot persistence**: each bounded context chooses the persistence technology that best serves its needs, within guardrails enforced by the Platform Team.\n\n1. **Event Store**  \n   *Technology:* Apache Kafka (log), TimescaleDB (cold storage)  \n   *Rationale:* Immutability, temporal queries, built-in replication.\n\n2. **User & Billing**  \n   *Technology:* PostgreSQL 15 with logical replication  \n   *Rationale:* Strong consistency for payments, GDPR data-subject requests.\n\n3. **Realtime Timelines & Notifications**  \n   *Technology:* Redis Cluster + Redis Streams  \n   *Rationale:* Sub-millisecond fan-out, pub/sub semantics, TTL-based eviction.\n\n4. **Search & Analytics**  \n   *Technology:* OpenSearch 2.x  \n   *Rationale:* Relevance scoring, aggregations, horizontal scaling.\n\n5. **Graph Recommendations**  \n   *Technology:* Neo4j 5.x  \n   *Rationale:* Native graph traversals for “students who liked X also liked Y”.\n\n6. **File Storage (Assignments, Media)**  \n   *Technology:* S3-compatible object store with signed URLs  \n   *Rationale:* Durable, CDN-friendly, off-loads traffic from app servers.\n\n### Access Pattern Enforcement  \n```mermaid\ngraph TD\n  subgraph Service Layer\n    SVC[Micro-service]\n  end\n  subgraph Repositories\n    REPO1[(PostgreSQL Repo)]\n    REPO2[(Redis Repo)]\n    REPO3[(Kafka Repo)]\n  end\n  SVC --> REPO1 & REPO2 & REPO3\n```\n\nRepositories shield business logic from vendor SDKs. All repositories implement:\n\n```ts\n// docs/examples/Repository.ts\nexport interface Repository<T> {\n  findById(id: string): Promise<T | null>;\n  save(entity: T): Promise<void>;\n  delete(id: string): Promise<void>;\n}\n```\n\nConcrete adapters (e.g., `UserPgRepository`, `TimelineRedisRepository`) live in their respective packages.\n\n### Data-Contracts & Async Sync  \nCross-store ownership is disallowed; instead we replicate immutable projections using Kafka topics suffixed with `.projection`. Consumers materialize local read models, guaranteeing eventual consistency while avoiding distributed transactions.\n\n## Consequences  \nPositive\n* Services scale independently; no single datastore becomes a bottleneck.\n* Teams can evolve schemas without cross-team migration battles.\n* Disaster recovery is narrowed to datastore-specific SLAs.\n\nNegative\n* Ops: multiple backup/restore, monitoring, and upgrade procedures.\n* Observability: needing distributed tracing across store boundaries.\n* Onboarding: developers must learn ≥ 3 query DSLs.\n\nMitigations\n* Unified secrets via Vault; IaC modules standardize provisioning.\n* Cross-store dashboards in Grafana/Loki/Tempo.\n* Internal workshops and cheat-sheets for new engineers.\n\n## Related Decisions  \n* ADR-001 – Event-Driven Architecture  \n* ADR-002 – Domain-Driven Repository Pattern\n\n## Notes  \nThis ADR will be revisited after the next academic year or when datastore OPEX exceeds 20 % of infrastructure budget.","numLines":108,"startLine":1,"totalLines":108}}}

The analysis traces the complete data flow through the system, identifies critical architectural components, and pinpoints the top three most likely causes of the delay.\n\n**Key Finding:** The latency is an inherent consequence of the system's event-driven, eventually consistent architecture as defined in ADR-001 and ADR-002. The delay is not a bug but a manifestation of the trade-offs made to achieve loose coupling, fault isolation, and horizontal scalability. However, specific architectural areas can be optimized to reduce the perceived latency.\n\n---\n\n## 1. End-to-End Data Flow Analysis\n\nThe user progress update flow consists of three distinct paths that operate on different timescales:\n\n### 1.1 The Write Path (Synchronous)\n\nWhen a user completes a lecture, the following synchronous sequence occurs:\n\n```\nUser Action (LecturePlayer.js)\n    │\n    ▼\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  FRONTEND (Synchronous API Calls)                                       │\n│  File: frontend/src/components/course/LecturePlayer.js:181-189          │\n│                                                                         │\n│  1. handleEnded() triggers when video ends                              │\n│  2. Redis lock for idempotency (5 second TTL)                         │\n│    2. Prisma DB write to lessonCompletion table                         │\n│    3. publishEvent(EventTopics.LESSON_COMPLETED, {...})                 │\n│    4. Cache invalidation: redis.del(`course:${courseId}:*`)             │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n    │\n    ▼ Kafka/NATS publish (fire-and-forget semantics)\n    │\n    └──► HTTP 200/201 returned to frontend (synchronous path complete)\n```\n\n**Critical Code Reference - Single broker (dev): kafka:9092                                    │\n│    - KAFKA_OFFSETS_TOPIC_REPLICATION_FACTOR: 1                          │\n│    - No explicit partitioning strategy visible                          │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n    │\n    ├──► Consumer: Gamification Service (points projection)\n    │    File: ADR-002, Section 4.4\n    │    - groupId: 'gamification-points-projector-v1'\n    │    - Subscribes to education.assignment topic\n    │    - Updates user points in read model\n    │\n    ├──► Consumer: Notification Service (WebSocket push)\n    │    Port 7003 (docker-compose.yml:246-256)\n    │    - Pushes 'ProgressUpdated' event via WebSocket\n    │\n    ├──► Consumer: Activity Service (activity stream)\n    │    Port 7002 (docker-compose.yml:235-243)\n    │    - Materializes activity feed read model\n    │\n    └──► Consumer: Search Indexer (Elasticsearch)\n         - Updates search indices for course progress\n```\n\n**Critical Code Reference - course-service/src/app.js:127-131:**\n```javascript\nasync function handleAssignmentSubmitted({ courseId, studentId }) {\n  await Course.update({ updatedAt: new Date() }, { where: { id: courseId } });\n  await publishEvent('CourseProgressUpdated', { courseId, studentId, ts: Date.now() });\n}\n```\n\n### 1.3 The Read Path (Frontend Data Retrieval)\n\nThe dashboard retrieves updated data through two mechanisms:\n\n#### Mechanism A: Initial REST API Fetch\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  DashboardPage.js (Initial Load + Manual Refresh)                       │\n│  File: frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.js:139-156                      │\n│                                                                         │\n│  fetchDashboardSnapshot():                                              │\n│    - GET /dashboard via DashboardService.getSnapshot()                  │\n│    - Returns: { metrics, activity, progress }                           │\n│    - Called ONLY on mount, no periodic polling                          │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n#### Mechanism B: WebSocket Real-Time Updates\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  DashboardPage.js (WebSocket Subscription)                              │\n│  File: frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.js:182-223                      │\n│                                                                         │\n│  initSocket():                                                          │\n│    - Connects to REACT_APP_WS_ENDPOINT via Socket.io                    │\n│    - Listens for 'dashboard-event' messages                             │\n│    - Event types handled:                                               │\n│        - 'BadgeAwarded' → updates badges count                          │\n│        - 'AssignmentSubmitted' → updates submission count               │\n│        - 'ProgressUpdated' → setProgressData(chartData)                 │\n│        - 'FeedItem' → prepends to activity feed                         │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n#### Mechanism C: ProgressChart WebSocket (Direct WS)\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  ProgressChart.js (Dedicated WebSocket)                                 │\n│  File: frontend/src/components/dashboard/ProgressChart.js:62-174        │\n│                                                                         │\n│  useProgressStream(userId):                                             │\n│    - GET /api/v1/activity-stream?page=0&size=25                         │\n│    - Supports infinite scroll pagination                                │\n│                                                                         │\n│  Real-Time Updates:                                                     │\n│    - WebSocket: ${WS_BASE}/activity?token=${token}                      │\n│    - Prepends new events to existing list                               │\n│    - De-duplicates by event ID                                          │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Key Architectural Components Involved\n\n### 2.1 Frontend Layer\n\n| Component | File Path | Role |\n|-----------|-----------|------|\n| LecturePlayer | `frontend/src/components/dashboard/ActivityStream.js` | Hybrid REST+WebSocket activity feed |\n| courseService | `frontend/src/services/courseService.js` | Axios HTTP client with caching (30s TTL) Cache | Redis 7 | `docker-compose.yml:92-104` |\n| Search | Elasticsearch 8.11.1 | `docker-compose.yml:153-168` |\n| Object Storage | MinIO | `docker-compose.yml:183-197` |\n\n### 2.4 Critical Code `services/payment-service/src/events/consumer.js` | Consumer pattern with retry and DLQ |\n| Event Publisher | `services/course-service/src/api/courseController.js:33` | Domain event dispatching from controller |\n\n---\n\n## 3. Top 3 Most Likely Architectural Areas Causing Delay\n\n### 3.1 Bottleneck #1: Kafka Consumer Lag Under Load\n\n**Justification:**\n\nThe event-driven architecture relies on Kafka for cross-service communication (ADR-002). The symptom of \"intermittent delay that worsens under peak usage\" is a classic indicator of **consumer lag** - where the rate of message production exceeds the consumer's processing capacity.\n\n**Evidence from Codebase:**\n\n1. **Single-Partition Configuration** (`docker-compose.yml:124`):\n   ```yaml\n   KAFKA_OFFSETS_TOPIC_REPLICATION_FACTOR: 1\n   ```\n   A replication factor of 1 in a development setup suggests a single broker with limited partition distribution. Under load, a single partition becomes a bottleneck because Kafka consumers in the same consumer group can only have one active consumer per partition.\n\n2. **Consumer Group Design** (`services/course-service/src/app.js:96`):\n   ```javascript\n   const consumer = kafka.consumer({ groupId: KAFKA_GROUP_ID });\n   ```\n   The `course-service-consumer` group ID is shared, meaning multiple service instances would compete for the same partitions rather than scaling horizontally.\n\n3. **No Explicit Partitioning Strategy** (`services/course-service/src/app.js:98-103`):\n   ```javascript\n   async function publishEvent(topic, payload) {\n     await producer.send({\n       topic,\n       messages: [{ key: payload.id || null, value: JSON.stringify(payload) }],\n     });\n   }\n   ```\n   The `key` is set to `payload.id` or `null`. When `null`, messages are round-robin distributed, but when set, they're partitioned by key. Inconsistent keying can lead to hot partitions.\n\n4. **ADR-002 Acknowledges This Trade-off**:\n   > \"Negative / Trade-offs: ... developers must understand eventual consistency.\"\n\n**Impact Analysis:**\n\nWhen many users complete lectures simultaneously (peak hours), the `LESSON_COMPLETED` events queue up in Kafka. The single consumer instance in the notification-service processes them sequentially, causing a backlog. The delay between event publication and WebSocket push directly correlates with this lag.\n\n**Metric to Monitor:** Consumer lag can be monitored via `kafka-consumer-groups.sh --describe` or Prometheus metrics (`kafka_consumer_group_lag`).\n\n---\n\n### 3.2 Bottleneck #2: Frontend Polling Strategy and Stale Cache\n\n**Justification:**\n\nThe dashboard components use multiple data retrieval strategies that are not optimally coordinated, leading to perceived latency even when the backend has the updated data.\n\n**Evidence from Codebase:**\n\n1. **No Automatic Refresh After Write** (`frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.js:139-156`):\n   ```javascript\n   const fetchDashboardSnapshot = useCallback(async () => {\n     // ...\n     const snapshot = await DashboardService.getSnapshot({ token, signal });\n     setMetrics(snapshot.metrics);\n     setActivityFeed(snapshot.activity);\n     setProgressData(snapshot.progress);\n   }, [token]);\n   ```\n   This function is called only on mount (`useEffect` at line 240-252). There is **no periodic polling** and no re-fetch after the user completes an action. The dashboard relies entirely on WebSocket pushes for updates.\n\n2. **WebSocket Event Handling is Selective** (`frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.js:194-221`):\n   ```javascript\n   socket.current.on('dashboard-event', (domainEvent) => {\n     switch (domainEvent.type) {\n       case 'BadgeAwarded':\n         // updates badges\n         break;\n       case 'ProgressUpdated':\n         setProgressData(domainEvent.payload.chartData);\n         break;\n       // ...\n     }\n   });\n   ```\n   The `ProgressUpdated` event must be explicitly emitted by the backend for the chart to update. If the backend's consumer chain fails to emit this event (e.g., due to consumer lag or a missed event), the chart remains stale.\n\n3. **ProgressChart Has Its Own WebSocket** (`frontend/src/components/dashboard/ProgressChart.js:96-100`):\n   ```javascript\n   const wsUrl = `wss://api.pulselearn.com/ws/progress/${encodeURIComponent(userId)}`;\n   const ws = new WebSocket(wsUrl);\n   ```\n   This is a **separate WebSocket connection** from the DashboardPage's Socket.io connection. The two are not synchronized. If one updates but the other doesn't, the user sees inconsistent state.\n\n4. **Frontend Service Caching** (`frontend/src/services/courseService.js:77-78`):\n   ```javascript\n   const COURSE_CACHE_TTL = 30 * 1000; // 30s\n   const courseCache = createTTLCache(COURSE_CACHE_TTL);\n   ```\n   The 30-second TTL cache means that even if a user navigates to a course details page immediately after completion, they may see stale data until the cache expires.\n\n**Impact Analysis:**\n\nA user completes a lecture, the write succeeds, but:\n1. The DashboardPage doesn't automatically re-fetch\n2. The WebSocket push may be delayed due to Kafka consumer lag\n3. Even if data is fetched, the courseService cache may return stale data\n\nThe user perceives no update for up to 60 seconds (combining consumer lag + cache TTL + WebSocket reconnection delay).\n\n---\n\n### 3.3 Bottleneck #3: Eventual Consistency and Read Model Projection Delay\n\n**Justification:**\n\nThe architecture explicitly uses **eventual consistency** with read model projections (ADR-001, ADR-002, ADR-003). The dashboard reads from materialized views that are updated asynchronously, not from the source of truth.\n\n**Evidence from Codebase:**\n\n1. **Read Models are Projections** (`docs/ADR/002-event-sourcing-with-kafka.md`):\n   > \"Append-only: write operations emit events; read models are derived via consumer projections.\"\n\n   The progress data shown on the dashboard is a **projection** (a denormalized read model) maintained by a consumer that listens to the event stream. This projection may be in a different database (Redis for timelines per ADR-003).\n\n2. **Polyglot Persistence** (`docs/ADR/003-polyglot-persistence.md`):\n   > \"Realtime Timelines & Notifications: Technology: Redis Cluster + Redis Streams. Rationale: Sub-millisecond fan-out, pub/sub semantics, TTL-based eviction.\"\n\n   The activity stream and progress chart may read from Redis projections, not PostgreSQL. The consumer that updates these projections must process the Kafka event first.\n\n3. **Cross-Store Synchronization** (`docs/ADR/003-polyglot-persistence.md:84-85`):\n   > \"Cross-store ownership is disallowed; instead we replicate immutable projections using Kafka topics suffixed with `.projection`. Consumers materialize local read models, guaranteeing eventual consistency while avoiding distributed transactions.\"\n\n4. **Consumer Projection Chain** (inferred from `services/course-service/src/app.js:127-131`):\n   ```javascript\n   async function handleAssignmentSubmitted({ courseId, studentId }) {\n     await Course.update({ updatedAt: new Date() }, { where: { id: courseId } });\n     await publishEvent('CourseProgressUpdated', { courseId, studentId, ts: Date.now() });\n   }\n   ```\n   This shows a **two-hop event chain**:\n   - `AssignmentSubmitted` → consumed by course-service\n   - `CourseProgressUpdated` → published for downstream consumers (notification-service, activity-service)\n\n   Each hop introduces latency from consumer processing and network round-trips.\n\n5. **DLQ and Retry Logic** (`services/payment-service/src/events/consumer.js:173-181`):\n   ```javascript\n   await pRetry(() => handler(data, metadata), {\n     retries: MAX_PROCESSING_RETRIES, // 5 retries\n     onFailedAttempt: (err) => {\n       logger.warn(`Attempt ${err.attemptNumber} failed...`);\n     },\n   });\n   ```\n   Retries introduce additional delays if messages fail on first attempt.\n\n**Impact Analysis:**\n\nThe total latency is the sum of:\n1. **Source write** (PostgreSQL lessonCompletion) - ~10-50ms\n2. **Event publish to Kafka** - ~5-20ms\n3. **Consumer pickup delay** (depends on lag) - 0ms to 10+ seconds under load\n4. **Projection update** (Redis/Elasticsearch write) - ~5-50ms\n5. **Secondary event publish** (CourseProgressUpdated) - ~5-20ms\n6. **Notification-service consumer pickup** - 0ms to 10+ seconds\n7. **WebSocket broadcast** - ~5-50ms\n\nUnder ideal conditions: ~50-200ms\nUnder load with consumer lag: 10-60+ seconds\n\n---\n\n## 4. Architectural Trade-off Analysis\n\nThe latency is not a defect but an **explicit trade-off** documented in the ADRs:\n\n| Trade-off | Benefit | Cost |\n|-----------|---------|------|\n| Event Sourcing (ADR-002) | Auditability, replayability, loose coupling | Eventual consistency, write complexity |\n| Microservices (ADR-001) | Independent deployability, fault isolation | Operational overhead, eventual consistency |\n| Polyglot Persistence (ADR-003) | Fit-for-purpose data stores | Cross-store synchronization delay |\n\n**Quote from ADR-001:**\n> \"Negative: ... Data consistency becomes eventual; we must adopt Saga patterns for workflows...\"\n\n**Quote from ADR-002:**\n> \"Negative / Trade-offs: ... developers must understand eventual consistency.\"\n\n---\n\n## 5. Recommendations\n\n### Short-term Mitigations (No Architecture Change)\n\n1. **Optimistic UI Updates**: After the `handleEnded()` POST returns successfully, immediately update the local React state to reflect the completion without waiting for the WebSocket push.\n\n2. **Increase Kafka Partitions**: Increase partitions for high-throughput topics to allow parallel consumption.\n\n3. **Add Periodic Polling Fallback**: Implement a 10-second polling interval in DashboardPage as a fallback when WebSocket events are delayed.\n\n4. **Reduce Frontend Cache TTL**: Reduce the courseService cache from 30s to 5-10s for progress-related endpoints.\n\n### Medium-term Optimizations\n\n1. **Consumer Scaling**: Deploy multiple instances of notification-service with unique consumer group assignments across partitions.\n\n2. **Read-Your-Writes Consistency**: After a write, include the expected version/timestamp in the response. The frontend can then poll until the read model matches this version.\n\n3. **Redis Streams for Real-time Path**: Bypass Kafka for user-facing real-time updates and use Redis Pub/Sub or Streams for sub-second delivery.\n\n### Long-term Architectural Considerations\n\n1. **CQRS Command Acknowledgment**: Implement a command/query acknowledgment pattern where the write path returns a correlation ID, and the frontend subscribes to a completion notification for that specific ID.\n\n2. **WebSocket Unification**: Consolidate the multiple WebSocket connections (Socket.io for dashboard-events, native WebSocket for ProgressChart) into a single multiplexed connection.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Conclusion\n\nThe intermittent 60-second delay in progress updates is a consequence of the PulseLearn Campus Hub's event-driven, eventually consistent architecture. The three primary bottlenecks are:\n\n1. **Kafka consumer lag** under peak load causing event processing delays\n2. **Frontend polling/caching strategy** that doesn't account for write-read latency\n3. **Multi-hop event chains** inherent in the CQRS projection pattern\n\nThese are not bugs but architectural trade-offs made to achieve scalability, fault tolerance, and loose coupling. The recommendations above provide a path to reducing perceived latency while preserving the benefits of the current architecture.\n\n---\n\n## Appendix: File Reference Summary\n\n| Purpose | File Path | Key Lines |\n|---------|-----------|-----------|\n| Write trigger | `frontend/src/components/course/LecturePlayer.js` | 181-189 |\n| Dashboard fetch | `frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.js` | 139-156, 182-223 |\n| Progress chart WS | `frontend/src/components/dashboard/ProgressChart.js` | 62-174 |\n| Activity stream | `frontend/src/components/dashboard/ActivityStream.js` | 85-208 |\n| Course service | `services/course-service/src/services/courseService.js` | 317-350, 72-93 |\n| App bootstrap | `services/course-service/src/app.js` | 96-131 |\n| Event consumer pattern | `services/payment-service/src/events/consumer.js` | 74-148 |\n| Kafka config | `docker-compose.yml` | 120-138 |\n| ADR Event Sourcing | `docs/ADR/002-event-sourcing-with-kafka.md` | Full document |\n| ADR Microservices | `docs/ADR/001-microservices-architecture.md` | Full document |\n| ADR Polyglot | `docs/ADR/003-polyglot-persistence.md` | Full document |\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":7753,"cache_read_input_tokens":81304,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":7753,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d9becd8e-6c54-4142-a57f-86d6a46bf7e3","uuid":"16602954-9257-4a61-bdad-b6a605c15fc8"}

The analysis traces the complete data flow through the system, identifies critical architectural components, and pinpoints the top three most likely causes of the delay.\n\n**Key Finding:** The latency is an inherent consequence of the system's event-driven, eventually consistent architecture as defined in ADR-001 and ADR-002. The delay is not a bug but a manifestation of the trade-offs made to achieve loose coupling, fault isolation, and horizontal scalability. However, specific architectural areas can be optimized to reduce the perceived latency.\n\n---\n\n## 1. End-to-End Data Flow Analysis\n\nThe user progress update flow consists of three distinct paths that operate on different timescales:\n\n### 1.1 The Write Path (Synchronous)\n\nWhen a user completes a lecture, the following synchronous sequence occurs:\n\n```\nUser Action (LecturePlayer.js)\n    │\n    ▼\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  FRONTEND (Synchronous API Calls)                                       │\n│  File: frontend/src/components/course/LecturePlayer.js:181-189          │\n│                                                                         │\n│  1. handleEnded() triggers when video ends                              │\n│  2. Emits 'LectureCompleted' event to local eventBus                    │\n│  3. POSTs to /courses/${courseId}/lectures/${lectureId}/complete        │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n    │\n    ▼ HTTP POST (via api.js → axios)\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  API GATEWAY (nginx reverse proxy)                                       │\n│  File: services/api-gateway/nginx.conf                                   │\n│                                                                         │\n│  - Routes /courses/* to content_service upstream                        │\n│  - Rate limiting: 600 req/min (configurable)                            │\n│  - SSL termination, security headers                                    │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n    │\n    ▼ Proxied to course-service\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  COURSE SERVICE (Business Logic)                                         │\n│  File: services/course-service/src/services/courseService.js:317-350    │\n│                                                                         │\n│  completeLesson(courseId, lessonId, userId):                            │\n│    1. Redis lock for idempotency (5 second TTL)                         │\n│    2. Prisma DB write to lessonCompletion table                         │\n│    3. publishEvent(EventTopics.LESSON_COMPLETED, {...})                 │\n│    4. Cache invalidation: redis.del(`course:${courseId}:*`)             │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n    │\n    ▼ Kafka/NATS publish (fire-and-forget semantics)\n    │\n    └──► HTTP 200/201 returned to frontend (synchronous path complete)\n```\n\n**Critical Code Reference - LecturePlayer.js:181-189:**\n```javascript\nconst handleEnded = async () => {\n  setPlaying(false);\n  emitEvent('LectureCompleted', { duration: roundTime(progress.playedSeconds) });\n  try {\n    await api.post(`/courses/${courseId}/lectures/${lectureId}/complete`);\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.error('Failed marking lecture complete', e);\n  }\n};\n```\n\n### 1.2 The Event Propagation Path (Asynchronous)\n\nAfter the synchronous write completes, the event enters the asynchronous messaging backbone:\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  KAFKA / NATS MESSAGE BROKER                                            │\n│  Configured in: docker-compose.yml:120-138, ADR-002                     │\n│                                                                         │\n│  Topics involved:                                                       │\n│    - education.assignment (ADR-002 naming convention)                   │\n│    - LESSON_COMPLETED (EventTopics constant)                            │\n│    - CourseProgressUpdated (downstream projection)                      │\n│                                                                         │\n│  Configuration:                                                         │\n│    - Single broker (dev): kafka:9092                                    │\n│    - KAFKA_OFFSETS_TOPIC_REPLICATION_FACTOR: 1                          │\n│    - No explicit partitioning strategy visible                          │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n    │\n    ├──► Consumer: Gamification Service (points projection)\n    │    File: ADR-002, Section 4.4\n    │    - groupId: 'gamification-points-projector-v1'\n    │    - Subscribes to education.assignment topic\n    │    - Updates user points in read model\n    │\n    ├──► Consumer: Notification Service (WebSocket push)\n    │    Port 7003 (docker-compose.yml:246-256)\n    │    - Pushes 'ProgressUpdated' event via WebSocket\n    │\n    ├──► Consumer: Activity Service (activity stream)\n    │    Port 7002 (docker-compose.yml:235-243)\n    │    - Materializes activity feed read model\n    │\n    └──► Consumer: Search Indexer (Elasticsearch)\n         - Updates search indices for course progress\n```\n\n**Critical Code Reference - course-service/src/app.js:127-131:**\n```javascript\nasync function handleAssignmentSubmitted({ courseId, studentId }) {\n  await Course.update({ updatedAt: new Date() }, { where: { id: courseId } });\n  await publishEvent('CourseProgressUpdated', { courseId, studentId, ts: Date.now() });\n}\n```\n\n### 1.3 The Read Path (Frontend Data Retrieval)\n\nThe dashboard retrieves updated data through two mechanisms:\n\n#### Mechanism A: Initial REST API Fetch\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  DashboardPage.js (Initial Load + Manual Refresh)                       │\n│  File: frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.js:139-156                      │\n│                                                                         │\n│  fetchDashboardSnapshot():                                              │\n│    - GET /dashboard via DashboardService.getSnapshot()                  │\n│    - Returns: { metrics, activity, progress }                           │\n│    - Called ONLY on mount, no periodic polling                          │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n#### Mechanism B: WebSocket Real-Time Updates\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  DashboardPage.js (WebSocket Subscription)                              │\n│  File: frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.js:182-223                      │\n│                                                                         │\n│  initSocket():                                                          │\n│    - Connects to REACT_APP_WS_ENDPOINT via Socket.io                    │\n│    - Listens for 'dashboard-event' messages                             │\n│    - Event types handled:                                               │\n│        - 'BadgeAwarded' → updates badges count                          │\n│        - 'AssignmentSubmitted' → updates submission count               │\n│        - 'ProgressUpdated' → setProgressData(chartData)                 │\n│        - 'FeedItem' → prepends to activity feed                         │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n#### Mechanism C: ProgressChart WebSocket (Direct WS)\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  ProgressChart.js (Dedicated WebSocket)                                 │\n│  File: frontend/src/components/dashboard/ProgressChart.js:62-174        │\n│                                                                         │\n│  useProgressStream(userId):                                             │\n│    - Opens WebSocket to wss://api.pulselearn.com/ws/progress/:userId    │\n│    - Handles 'message' events for progress updates                      │\n│    - Implements exponential backoff reconnection                        │\n│        INITIAL_RECONNECT_INTERVAL: 1000ms                               │\n│        MAX_RECONNECT_INTERVAL: 30000ms                                  │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n#### Mechanism D: ActivityStream (REST + WebSocket Hybrid)\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  ActivityStream.js (Hybrid Approach)                                    │\n│  File: frontend/src/components/dashboard/ActivityStream.js:85-208       │\n│                                                                         │\n│  Initial Load:                                                          │\n│    - GET /api/v1/activity-stream?page=0&size=25                         │\n│    - Supports infinite scroll pagination                                │\n│                                                                         │\n│  Real-Time Updates:                                                     │\n│    - WebSocket: ${WS_BASE}/activity?token=${token}                      │\n│    - Prepends new events to existing list                               │\n│    - De-duplicates by event ID                                          │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Key Architectural Components Involved\n\n### 2.1 Frontend Layer\n\n| Component | File Path | Role |\n|-----------|-----------|------|\n| LecturePlayer | WebSocket subscription |\n| ProgressChart | `frontend/src/components/dashboard/ProgressChart.js` | Dedicated WebSocket for course completion percentages |\n| ActivityStream | `frontend/src/components/dashboard/ActivityStream.js` | Hybrid REST+WebSocket activity feed |\n| courseService | `frontend/src/services/courseService.js` | Axios HTTP client with caching (30s TTL) Cache | Redis 7 | `docker-compose.yml:92-104` |\n| Search | Elasticsearch 8.11.1 | `docker-compose.yml:153-168` |\n| Object Storage | MinIO | `docker-compose.yml:183-197` |\n\n### 2.4 Critical Code `services/payment-service/src/events/consumer.js` | Consumer pattern with retry and DLQ |\n| Event Publisher | `services/course-service/src/api/courseController.js:33` | Domain event dispatching from controller |\n\n---\n\n## 3. Top 3 Most Likely Architectural Areas Causing Delay\n\n### 3.1 Bottleneck #1: Kafka Consumer Lag Under Load\n\n**Justification:**\n\nThe event-driven architecture relies on Kafka for cross-service communication (ADR-002). The symptom of \"intermittent delay that worsens under peak usage\" is a classic indicator of **consumer lag** - where the rate of message production exceeds the consumer's processing capacity.\n\n**Evidence from Codebase:**\n\n1. **Single-Partition Configuration** (`docker-compose.yml:124`):\n   ```yaml\n   KAFKA_OFFSETS_TOPIC_REPLICATION_FACTOR: 1\n   ```\n   A replication factor of 1 in a development setup suggests a single broker with limited partition distribution. Under load, a single partition becomes a bottleneck because Kafka consumers in the same consumer group can only have one active consumer per partition.\n\n2. **Consumer Group Design** (`services/course-service/src/app.js:96`):\n   ```javascript\n   const consumer = kafka.consumer({ groupId: KAFKA_GROUP_ID });\n   ```\n   The `course-service-consumer` group ID is shared, meaning multiple service instances would compete for the same partitions rather than scaling horizontally.\n\n3. **No Explicit Partitioning Strategy** (`services/course-service/src/app.js:98-103`):\n   ```javascript\n   async function publishEvent(topic, payload) {\n     await producer.send({\n       topic,\n       messages: [{ key: payload.id || null, value: JSON.stringify(payload) }],\n     });\n   }\n   ```\n   The `key` is set to `payload.id` or `null`. When `null`, messages are round-robin distributed, but when set, they're partitioned by key. Inconsistent keying can lead to hot partitions.\n\n4. **ADR-002 Acknowledges This Trade-off**:\n   > \"Negative / Trade-offs: ... developers must understand eventual consistency.\"\n\n**Impact Analysis:**\n\nWhen many users complete lectures simultaneously (peak hours), the `LESSON_COMPLETED` events queue up in Kafka. The single consumer instance in the notification-service processes them sequentially, causing a backlog. The delay between event publication and WebSocket push directly correlates with this lag.\n\n**Metric to Monitor:** Consumer lag can be monitored via `kafka-consumer-groups.sh --describe` or Prometheus metrics (`kafka_consumer_group_lag`).\n\n---\n\n### 3.2 Bottleneck #2: Frontend Polling Strategy and Stale Cache\n\n**Justification:**\n\nThe dashboard components use multiple data retrieval strategies that are not optimally coordinated, leading to perceived latency even when the backend has the updated data.\n\n**Evidence from Codebase:**\n\n1. **No Automatic Refresh After Write** (`frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.js:139-156`):\n   ```javascript\n   const fetchDashboardSnapshot = useCallback(async () => {\n     // ...\n     const snapshot = await DashboardService.getSnapshot({ token, signal });\n     setMetrics(snapshot.metrics);\n     setActivityFeed(snapshot.activity);\n     setProgressData(snapshot.progress);\n   }, [token]);\n   ```\n   This function is called only on mount (`useEffect` at line 240-252). There is **no periodic polling** and no re-fetch after the user completes an action. The dashboard relies entirely on WebSocket pushes for updates.\n\n2. **WebSocket Event Handling is Selective** (`frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.js:194-221`):\n   ```javascript\n   socket.current.on('dashboard-event', (domainEvent) => {\n     switch (domainEvent.type) {\n       case 'BadgeAwarded':\n         // updates badges\n         break;\n       case 'ProgressUpdated':\n         setProgressData(domainEvent.payload.chartData);\n         break;\n       // ...\n     }\n   });\n   ```\n   The `ProgressUpdated` event must be explicitly emitted by the backend for the chart to update. If the backend's consumer chain fails to emit this event (e.g., due to consumer lag or a missed event), the chart remains stale.\n\n3. **ProgressChart Has Its Own WebSocket** (`frontend/src/components/dashboard/ProgressChart.js:96-100`):\n   ```javascript\n   const wsUrl = `wss://api.pulselearn.com/ws/progress/${encodeURIComponent(userId)}`;\n   const ws = new WebSocket(wsUrl);\n   ```\n   This is a **separate WebSocket connection** from the DashboardPage's Socket.io connection. The two are not synchronized. If one updates but the other doesn't, the user sees inconsistent state.\n\n4. **Frontend Service Caching** (`frontend/src/services/courseService.js:77-78`):\n   ```javascript\n   const COURSE_CACHE_TTL = 30 * 1000; // 30s\n   const courseCache = createTTLCache(COURSE_CACHE_TTL);\n   ```\n   The 30-second TTL cache means that even if a user navigates to a course details page immediately after completion, they may see stale data until the cache expires.\n\n**Impact Analysis:**\n\nA user completes a lecture, the write succeeds, but:\n1. The DashboardPage doesn't automatically re-fetch\n2. The WebSocket push may be delayed due to Kafka consumer lag\n3. Even if data is fetched, the courseService cache may return stale data\n\nThe user perceives no update for up to 60 seconds (combining consumer lag + cache TTL + WebSocket reconnection delay).\n\n---\n\n### 3.3 Bottleneck #3: Eventual Consistency and Read Model Projection Delay\n\n**Justification:**\n\nThe architecture explicitly uses **eventual consistency** with read model projections (ADR-001, ADR-002, ADR-003). The dashboard reads from materialized views that are updated asynchronously, not from the source of truth.\n\n**Evidence from Codebase:**\n\n1. **Read Models are Projections** (`docs/ADR/002-event-sourcing-with-kafka.md`):\n   > \"Append-only: write operations emit events; read models are derived via consumer projections.\"\n\n   The progress data shown on the dashboard is a **projection** (a denormalized read model) maintained by a consumer that listens to the event stream. This projection may be in a different database (Redis for timelines per ADR-003).\n\n2. **Polyglot Persistence** (`docs/ADR/003-polyglot-persistence.md`):\n   > \"Realtime Timelines & Notifications: Technology: Redis Cluster + Redis Streams. Rationale: Sub-millisecond fan-out, pub/sub semantics, TTL-based eviction.\"\n\n   The activity stream and progress chart may read from Redis projections, not PostgreSQL. The consumer that updates these projections must process the Kafka event first.\n\n3. **Cross-Store Synchronization** (`docs/ADR/003-polyglot-persistence.md:84-85`):\n   > \"Cross-store ownership is disallowed; instead we replicate immutable projections using Kafka topics suffixed with `.projection`. Consumers materialize local read models, guaranteeing eventual consistency while avoiding distributed transactions.\"\n\n4. **Consumer Projection Chain** (inferred from `services/course-service/src/app.js:127-131`):\n   ```javascript\n   async function handleAssignmentSubmitted({ courseId, studentId }) {\n     await Course.update({ updatedAt: new Date() }, { where: { id: courseId } });\n     await publishEvent('CourseProgressUpdated', { courseId, studentId, ts: Date.now() });\n   }\n   ```\n   This shows a **two-hop event chain**:\n   - `AssignmentSubmitted` → consumed by course-service\n   - `CourseProgressUpdated` → published for downstream consumers (notification-service, activity-service)\n\n   Each hop introduces latency from consumer processing and network round-trips.\n\n5. **DLQ and Retry Logic** (`services/payment-service/src/events/consumer.js:173-181`):\n   ```javascript\n   await pRetry(() => handler(data, metadata), {\n     retries: MAX_PROCESSING_RETRIES, // 5 retries\n     onFailedAttempt: (err) => {\n       logger.warn(`Attempt ${err.attemptNumber} failed...`);\n     },\n   });\n   ```\n   Retries introduce additional delays if messages fail on first attempt.\n\n**Impact Analysis:**\n\nThe total latency is the sum of:\n1. **Source write** (PostgreSQL lessonCompletion) - ~10-50ms\n2. **Event publish to Kafka** - ~5-20ms\n3. **Consumer pickup delay** (depends on lag) - 0ms to 10+ seconds under load\n4. **Projection update** (Redis/Elasticsearch write) - ~5-50ms\n5. **Secondary event publish** (CourseProgressUpdated) - ~5-20ms\n6. **Notification-service consumer pickup** - 0ms to 10+ seconds\n7. **WebSocket broadcast** - ~5-50ms\n\nUnder ideal conditions: ~50-200ms\nUnder load with consumer lag: 10-60+ seconds\n\n---\n\n## 4. Architectural Trade-off Analysis\n\nThe latency is not a defect but an **explicit trade-off** documented in the ADRs:\n\n| Trade-off | Benefit | Cost |\n|-----------|---------|------|\n| Event Sourcing (ADR-002) | Auditability, replayability, loose coupling | Eventual consistency, write complexity |\n| Microservices (ADR-001) | Independent deployability, fault isolation | Operational overhead, eventual consistency |\n| Polyglot Persistence (ADR-003) | Fit-for-purpose data stores | Cross-store synchronization delay |\n\n**Quote from ADR-001:**\n> \"Negative: ... Data consistency becomes eventual; we must adopt Saga patterns for workflows...\"\n\n**Quote from ADR-002:**\n> \"Negative / Trade-offs: ... developers must understand eventual consistency.\"\n\n---\n\n## 5. Recommendations\n\n### Short-term Mitigations (No Architecture Change)\n\n1. **Optimistic UI Updates**: After the `handleEnded()` POST returns successfully, immediately update the local React state to reflect the completion without waiting for the WebSocket push.\n\n2. **Increase Kafka Partitions**: Increase partitions for high-throughput topics to allow parallel consumption.\n\n3. **Add Periodic Polling Fallback**: Implement a 10-second polling interval in DashboardPage as a fallback when WebSocket events are delayed.\n\n4. **Reduce Frontend Cache TTL**: Reduce the courseService cache from 30s to 5-10s for progress-related endpoints.\n\n### Medium-term Optimizations\n\n1. **Consumer Scaling**: Deploy multiple instances of notification-service with unique consumer group assignments across partitions.\n\n2. **Read-Your-Writes Consistency**: After a write, include the expected version/timestamp in the response. The frontend can then poll until the read model matches this version.\n\n3. **Redis Streams for Real-time Path**: Bypass Kafka for user-facing real-time updates and use Redis Pub/Sub or Streams for sub-second delivery.\n\n### Long-term Architectural Considerations\n\n1. **CQRS Command Acknowledgment**: Implement a command/query acknowledgment pattern where the write path returns a correlation ID, and the frontend subscribes to a completion notification for that specific ID.\n\n2. **WebSocket Unification**: Consolidate the multiple WebSocket connections (Socket.io for dashboard-events, native WebSocket for ProgressChart) into a single multiplexed connection.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Conclusion\n\nThe intermittent 60-second delay in progress updates is a consequence of the PulseLearn Campus Hub's event-driven, eventually consistent architecture. The three primary bottlenecks are:\n\n1. **Kafka consumer lag** under peak load causing event processing delays\n2. **Frontend polling/caching strategy** that doesn't account for write-read latency\n3. **Multi-hop event chains** inherent in the CQRS projection pattern\n\nThese are not bugs but architectural trade-offs made to achieve scalability, fault tolerance, and loose coupling. The recommendations above provide a path to reducing perceived latency while preserving the benefits of the current architecture.\n\n---\n\n## Appendix: File Reference Summary\n\n| Purpose | File Path | Key Lines |\n|---------|-----------|-----------|\n| Write trigger | `frontend/src/components/course/LecturePlayer.js` | 181-189 |\n| Dashboard fetch | `frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.js` | 139-156, 182-223 |\n| Progress chart WS | `frontend/src/components/dashboard/ProgressChart.js` | 62-174 |\n| Activity stream | `frontend/src/components/dashboard/ActivityStream.js` | 85-208 |\n| Course service | `services/course-service/src/services/courseService.js` | 317-350, 72-93 |\n| App bootstrap | `services/course-service/src/app.js` | 96-131 |\n| Event consumer pattern | `services/payment-service/src/events/consumer.js` | 74-148 |\n| Kafka config | `docker-compose.yml` | 120-138 |\n| ADR Event Sourcing | `docs/ADR/002-event-sourcing-with-kafka.md` | Full document |\n| ADR Microservices | `docs/ADR/001-microservices-architecture.md` | Full document |\n| ADR Polyglot | `docs/ADR/003-polyglot-persistence.md` | Full document |\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
